## Document 316: Cliff Notes_ risks mount (2026-11-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ risks mount (2026-11-24).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: risks mount
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
10:31 November 20 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October, employment falling 43.5k following
September’s 36.4k decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the year have now slowed
to 1.1%yr, materially below current growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the

unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9% despite the participation rate falling
another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

### Page 2

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer momentum in hiring, now we are seeing
evidence of a material increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have remained in
employment to date, but persistent job shedding raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend
in the unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most
certainly before the late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3
increase was skewed towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay outcomes directly
tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements
meanwhile, the most responsive to current and expected labour market conditions, accounted for their
smallest share of a Q3 wage increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour cost

growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident in housing. The Westpac Housing
Sentiment Index fell 2.2% to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005. Interestingly,
there remains considerable latent demand: almost 13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over
the next year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak sentiment, high interest rates and

expectations of further price declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These factors also
pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for inflation remained the critical theme. The
October FOMC minutes highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs and energy
prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several [members] judg[ing] that price pressures were
increasingly extending beyond industries directly affected by data center construction”. Participants

"assessed that medium- and longer-term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-target inflation is a threat to the status
quo, and perceived risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke throughout the week
also kept the focus on price risks, and called for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again
proposed a 50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at the December and January

meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales
gave further justification for this view this week, the control group surprising to the upside with a 0.6%
gain in October after a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said, housing is under
considerable pressure, pending home sales falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back

at 2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent weakness in this sector will, in time, have
implications for both consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international development of the week. Again, it provided
a call for pro-active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr
and the decline in fixed asset investment broadened outside property, with capital expenditure now

down 8.1% across the economy year-to-date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth; though, even here, momentum softened
in October, annual growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not only activity that

### Page 3

Related articles
remains under pressure, but also wealth, with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective, stimulus needs to affect change in both activity
and wealth and do so in a sustainable manner.

## Document 348: Global Equity Strategy_ 2027 Outlook_ Finding Value in Growth

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Equity Strategy_ 2027 Outlook_ Finding Value in Growth.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 20
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 was the year of strong earnings. Global equity returns were unusually n
broad-based across regions, supported primarily by robust earnings growth — 
valuations have moderated. 
Earnings growth is poised to slow. As we enter 2027, earnings growth is on n
course to slow sharply across all major regions, growth in AI capex spending is 
likely to peak and the cost of capital remains elevated ... 
... But equities have de-rated sharply; with more attractive valuations, n
positive but slower nominal economic and earnings growth, and lower bond 
yields, inflation and oil prices, we expect equities to continue to rise, but 
with high volatility. 
We focus on Growth balanced with selective Defensives and beneficiaries of n
lower bond yields. 
Attractive opportunities are emerging in long-duration Growth companies, n
which should become more valuable as growth rates slow, particularly US 
Hyperscalers, which continue to enjoy strong (although slower) earnings growth 
and balance sheets. 
We balance this with selective defensive areas that offer reasonable valuations n
and areas that benefit from potential lower bond yields and oil prices. 
While forecast returns are highest in Asia, we focus on risk-adjusted returns and n
are overweight the US and Japan, neutral in Asia and EM, and underweight 
Europe, reflecting relative growth, profitability and valuation opportunities. 
Risks are becoming more two-sided. Bonds, oil prices and AI capex shifts could n
all increase volatility. We recommend diversification across Growth and 
selective Defensive strategies.

### Page 2

Value in Defensive Growth as earnings slow

Our theme for 2026 focused on a broadening equity bull market, with greater 
participation across regions, sectors and themes. Since then, the equity bull market has 
continued, and has widened out across the major regions despite rising oil prices and 
bond yields. The main driver has been strong corporate earnings growth across all 
regions. As we look to 2027, profit and AI capex growth look poised to slow in most 
regions. Nevertheless, recent price action has reflected this slowdown and equity 
valuations have come down substantially, particularly in the Technology sector. 
With economic activity and earnings on course to grow in 2027, albeit at a slower pace 
in nominal terms, and given the prospect of moderating inflation, bond yields and oil 
prices, we expect the equity bull market to continue. The range of potential outcomes 
has widened and, given stock concentration in the US and Asia, is particularly 
dependent on AI capex progress. Our recommended weightings reflect our 
assessment of risk-adjusted returns. We focus on: 
Growth — as earnings growth rates slow and margins moderate, investors are n
likely to reward these companies more highly, particularly as valuations become 
more attractive. AI-related companies have de-rated sharply, reflecting anxiety 
about peak AI capex spend and the possibility of slower returns. This is leaving 
attractive entry points, particularly in large capex Hyperscalers, which have strong 
balance sheets and diversified income streams. Significant upside potential has also 
opened up in more cyclical growth areas in memory and chips, although this comes 
with much higher risks and likely volatility.  
On a risk-adjusted basis, we are overweight the US and Japan, neutral Asia and n
EM, and underweight Europe.  
2026 — the year of earnings 
Key developments in 2026 form the foundations for a shift in opportunities in 2027. 
We see three standout features of global equity markets in 2026. 
Diversification has worked. Equity returns have been reasonable and very 1.
similar across all regions — reversing the trend between 2009 and 2025 when 
equity returns were consistently dominated by US outperformance. 
Strong earnings growth has been the principal driver of equity returns. 2.
All equity markets have de-rated, experiencing lower valuations. 3.
1. Diversified returns 
Overall, the past 12 months have seen broad-based returns across the regions. As 
Exhibit 1 shows, most equity markets have generated very similar total returns (the 
orange diamonds). This reverses the trend that had dominated post financial crisis 
through to 2025, when the US consistently outperformed other regions, benefiting from 
a combination of strong earnings growth and re-rating (as most of the businesses at the 
time were capital-light and high ROE). 
12 November 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Equity Strategy

### Page 3

2. Earnings have been the main driver of returns 
Earnings revisions have also been strong across the board (Exhibit 2). There are broadly 
three key drivers of this strength. First, Technology earnings have continued to grow 
robustly; second, higher oil prices have boosted earnings across the commodity 
sector; and third, the spill-over effects of the Technology capex boom have 
generally boosted earnings in HALO (heavy asset, low obsolescence) industries, 
such as Industrials. These factors have also contributed to an unusual pattern of 
earnings upgrades for 2026, 2027. So far, despite the recent equity sell-off, earnings 
revisions have also increased for 2028.

### Page 4

While this has been true in every region, the strongest upgrades have come in EM, largely 
reflecting huge upgrades in the Semiconductor space. This matters, because outsized 
gains in Tech Hardware stocks have pushed EM index concentration to record highs — 
Korea and Taiwan now account for about half of the MSCI EM, with Info Tech at >40% 
and the top 3 stocks (the ‘AI trio’ of TSMC, Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix) at ~30%. 
The recent sell-off in chip names, reflecting fears of a slower roll-out of data centres, has 
pushed valuations down sharply, creating good upside convexity — although the range 
of outcomes is wide and highly dependent on the pace of AI capex growth. See from our 
Equity analysts: At the center of tensions around the AI datacenter build: Searching for value 
following the sector-wide pullback. 
3. Equity markets have de-rated 
Equity valuations have fallen across the board. While the US equity market remains the 
most expensive, it has moderated from close to its 100th percentile over 20 years to a 
P/E of 18.5x.  Asian markets and EM are now trading at P/E ratios well below their 
historical averages. Overall, P/E ratios are around their average over the past 35 years, 
and a similar pattern is true of PEG ratios (adjusting valuations for expected growth) 
(Exhibit 3). Of course, much of the fall in valuations, particularly in the 
Tech-concentrated US and North Asia equity markets, has been the principal driver of 
this de-rating.

### Page 5

The key drivers for 2027 
Equity markets typically rise so long as nominal GDP is growing, valuations are not 
excessive, and interest rates are not rising sharply.  Nevertheless, the momentum of 
growth matters. The shift lower in equity markets in recent weeks — centred around AI 
concerns — has rapidly priced in the prospect of weaker growth rates, and the sharp 
sector and factor rotations within equities have pushed up the valuation of Defensive 
and Value parts of the market. From here, we expect equities to generate positive 
returns (Exhibit 4), albeit with higher volatility than in recent years. We expect investors 
to seek companies that can sustain growth as it becomes more scarce.

Theme 1: Earnings growth is on course to slow 
Our economists expect a slowing in nominal GDP growth rates in 2027 as the AI 
investment boom peaks. At the same time, nearly all markets are likely to see their 
earnings growth rates halve at least (Exhibit 5): in the US, slowing from 36% to 11%, 
Europe from just around 18% to 7%, Japan from 24% to 9% and Asia from close to

Why should earnings growth slow down? 
(a) Hyperscaler capex growth should slow, which should result in decelerating 
earnings growth (Exhibit 6) for much of the AI infrastructure complex and weaken the 
support for some of the ‘picks and shovels’ of the AI-related capex boom, including 
Industrials. Consensus estimates show a slowdown in IT earnings growth from close to 
90% in 2026 to 45% in 2027 and expect Communication Services to slow from growth of 
34% in 2026 to -5% in 2027. (b) Furthermore, depreciation charges should rise 
alongside other costs, such as interest charges. Together, headwinds to growth should 
boost the attractiveness of companies that can sustain higher growth and stable 
margins. This would be relatively good for the US Hyperscalers (whose valuations have 
fallen), but raises more risks for the chip names and Industrial Cyclicals, which have 
enjoyed the benefit of accelerating AI-related capex.

### Page 8

Theme 2: Cost of capital is higher 
Why is the cost of capital higher? 
First, higher inflation has pushed up policy rates. Our rates strategists expect to see a 1.
modest tightening cycle, but slow progress towards cutting interest rates in 2027. 
Second, we have argued that this has reflected the combined impact of a surge in 2.
private sector demand for capital and rising government borrowing. Our bond 
strategists do see some relief in longer-term rates, which should be more supportive 
for equities (Exhibit 8) but our credit strategists expect credit spreads to widen from 
current levels.

Investment Themes 
Broadly, we would focus on three areas within equities: Growth, selective Defensives and 
beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond yields. The expressions that best fit these 
themes vary somewhat across regions and there is some overlap, particularly between 
defensive areas and bond beneficiaries. Exhibit 9 shows a high level summary. More 
details can be found in the specific regional outlook reports.

### Page 9

Growth 
1) Growth versus Value 
For most of the period after the financial crisis through to the end of Covid-19, equity 
markets were dominated by Growth companies. A combination of surging technology 
profitability and ultra-low interest rates favoured Growth. At the same time, 
disinflationary pressures coupled with low energy prices and poor Banks returns 
(particularly in Europe), became headwinds for Value. Since 2022, however, the 
patterns have changed. Growth has still done well, but rising inflation and interest rates, 
coupled with higher energy prices and a capex boom, have strengthened the 
performance of many Value sectors. In all regions Value has sharply outperformed 
Growth in recent months (Exhibit 10).

Exhibit 9: We focus on three areas: Growth, selective Defensives and beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond yields

earlier turn in the AI cycle argue for greater portfolio resilience. Bond yields should 
decline modestly, but credit spreads could widen as growth slows. However, Cyclicals

(Exhibit 16).

We favour selective Defensives rather than a broad defensive shift.  We focus on 
defensive sectors that would also benefit from lower bond yields. Utilities and Telecoms 
in Europe, Healthcare and companies consistently buying back stock in the US, and Asian 
power companies are examples. Quality should benefit from slower growth and tighter 
financial conditions, but valuation discipline remains important. High dispersion should 
create a better environment for stock-picking, with a focus on stable earnings, structural 
growth, strong balance sheets and shareholder returns. Areas of focus include: US—
Healthcare, Power and Buyback Aristocrats; Asia—Shareholder-return strategies, 
secure dividends, strong balance sheets and companies with high and stable 
margins. In Europe, we would include Telecoms and Utilities. 
Investment focus: Selective beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond 
yields 
Some normalisation in the Middle East and a recovery in Persian Gulf supply should 
reduce geopolitical risk premia and bring down commodity prices, including European 
gas prices. Our commodities team forecast that Brent will fall from around $100/bbl at 
end-2026 to $78/bbl at end-2027, turning the energy-price impulse from a 0.4pp drag 
on global growth into a modest boost in 2027. Combined with fading US tariff effects 
and easing AI-related supply pressures, this should lower inflation and help central 
banks bring it back towards target. The Fed and ECB should move towards rate cuts in 
late 2027, supporting modestly lower bond yields. Our rates forecasts remain more 
dovish than market pricing.  
Investment implications. Lower inflation and bond yields should support long-duration 
Growth and rate-sensitive sectors. Falling energy costs should strengthen consumers’

favour Telecoms, Utilities, UK Real Estate, the DAX and Retailers. 
Overweight the US  
While the US equity market outperformed consistently over the 15 years post the 
financial crisis, its relative performance has faded since 2025. Over the course of 2026, 
the US equity market has seen more of a valuation squeeze than other markets; its ROE 
has increased relative to the rest of the world, while its relative P/B has contracted 
(Exhibit 17). Overall, we see US equity valuations as attractive versus history and also 
compared with other markets when adjusted for growth and likely volatility. We are 
underweight Europe; while lower oil prices would provide an important boost for 
European economies, it would also have a negative impact on earnings growth. 
Valuations have not fallen as much as in other regions, and the risks are higher given 
government debt issues and the heavy political calendar.

The devaluation of emerging market equities has been particularly notable through 
2026 (Exhibit 18). Driven by the AI investment boom, outsized gains in Tech Hardware 
stocks have pushed EM index concentration to record highs — Korea and Taiwan now 
account for about half of the MSCI EM, with Info Tech at >40% and the top 3 stocks (the 
‘AI trio’ of TSMC, Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix) at ~30%. Although the recent 
sell-off leaves room for a potential strong recovery, the risks remain higher than for the 
Hyperscalers. For MXAPJ (incl. Korea/Taiwan), we forecast a 17% USD price return and 
20% total return, supported by 26%/13% EPS growth in 2027/28 and a 9.3x target P/E, 
but Ex-Korea/Taiwan, we forecast 12% USD price return and 15% total return, and

across MXAPJ markets, the median USD total return is 13%, with an average of 15%. 
Given YTD annualised volatility of 24.2% for MXAPJ we recommend a neutral overall 
position for Asia x Japan and EM on a volatility-adjusted basis.

What are the risks? 
We see four in particular: bond volatility, oil volatility, weaker AI capex, and momentum 
concentration — each of which could generate both downside as well as upside risks for 
investors.  
1. The bond market 
The rise in bond yields through 2026 has, so far, largely been offset in equity markets by 
strong earnings growth. That said, it has contributed to lower P/E ratios, and the 
underperformance of sectors that are most sensitive to interest rates, such as Real 
Estate and Defensives, while contributing to the relative strength (until recently) of 
Cyclicals.   
Any relief in bond markets, leading to lower yields, would hamper the relative 
performance of Cyclicals and Financials and favour some of the more defensive 
areas of the equity markets — another reason to have some exposure to selective 
defensives and also sectors that are highly negatively correlated with bond yields.  
Sovereign debt concerns could result in a further spike in bond yields, with the risks 
highest in Europe during an election-heavy year, while an alleviation of inflationary 
pressures might lead to a rally.  
2. Oil prices  
The combination of rising oil prices and bond yields has been central to the movement in 
equity markets through 2026. Our commodity analysts forecast oil at $88/bbl for Brent

### Page 17

and $78/bbl for WTI in 2027, with risks in both directions. In general, lower energy
prices would be good for equity markets, bringing down inflation and interest rate
expectations. On balance, we think this would boost longer duration growth valuations
(from the relatively depressed levels currently), while also supporting defensive growth.
By contrast, such an outcome would reduce profit growth expectations for Energy
companies — a factor that would likely weigh more on Europe given that Energy is a

relatively important contributor to earnings growth.
3. AI capex spend
AI capex spending has been an important support for equity markets over the past three
years, driving earnings growth across the Technology ecosystem, and beyond into the
capex beneficiaries. Our economists expect the AI capex boom to slow. This could lead
to a de-rating in sectors that have become the beneficiaries of the capex boom,
including parts of the Industrials sector, which has re-rated, but also memory and chips

stocks. The latter have de-rated sharply, making the entry point more attractive, but
volatility is likely to remain high.
4. Narrow Breadth & Concentration
Related to this point, the US and Asia equity markets have a high degree of stock
concentration in their indices. Narrow breadth can create more stock volatility, with the
dominant stocks rising or the rest of the market falling. Either way, the momentum factor
risks high volatility and having some diversification across Growth and some Defensives

should help to improve risk-adjusted returns.  From a geographic perspective, the two
most concentrated regions are the US and Asia. That said, given recent market action,
we think the large cap technology names in the US are more defensive that those in Asia.
On a risk-adjusted basis, therefore, we think the momentum and concentration risks are
higher in Asia and imply lower risk-adjusted returns.

整体债务融资规模最⾼约 40 亿美元，较此前 75 亿美元⽬标缩⽔近半，资⾦将主要⽤于采购
英伟达 GB300 芯⽚，推进印尼数据中⼼建设。 AI ⾏业估值担忧升温、全球借贷成本⾼企，
正使相关基础设施项⽬⾯临更趋审慎的融资环境。
摘要：
英伟达⽀持的⼈⼯智能公司 Firmus 在搁置 IPO 计划后，正转向债务市场融资，以推进其在印度尼
西亚的数据中⼼项⽬。受 AI ⾏业估值承压及融资环境趋紧影响，该公司融资规模明显收缩，最新
债务融资⽬标较两个⽉前提出的⽅案⼤幅缩⽔。
11 ⽉ 23 ⽇，据彭博援引知情⼈⼠， Firmus 正考虑发⾏约 15 亿美元债券，作为⼀项总规模最⾼约
40 亿美元的综合债务融资⽅案的⼀部分。该⽅案还包括银⾏贷款和夹层债务，较公司两个⽉前
设定的约 75 亿美元融资⽬标减少近⼀半。Firmus 最快可能在今年年底前完成债券发⾏，但具体

时间仍取决于市场环境及其他条件。
融资所得将主要⽤于为 Firmus 与 DayOne Data Centers 合作开发的印尼数据中⼼采购英伟达最
新款 GB300 AI 芯⽚。在 AI 基础设施投资持续扩⼤的同时，市场对相关企业的估值和融资⻛险⽇
趋谨慎， Firmus 融资计划的调整也反映出这⼀变化。
IPO 受阻，转向债务融资
Firmus 上⽉搁置了 IPO 计划。此前，该公司原计划通过上市募资最⾼ 55 亿美元（含超额配售
权）。上市计划受阻后，公司转⽽寻求债务融资，以满⾜数据中⼼项⽬的资⾦需求。
报道援引知情⼈⼠称， Firmus 最近⼀周仍在与银⾏及投资者磋商融资⽅案。拟议中的债务融资
包括优先级债务和夹层债务，其中约 15 亿美元债券将与配套银⾏贷款共同构成优先级融资部
分。
债券和银⾏贷款的期限均为 69 个⽉，预计享有同等受偿顺序。债券的潜在收益率约为 8% ，银⾏
贷款利率则预计为 SOFR （有担保隔夜融资利率）加 325 个基点。
信⽤评级⽅⾯， Firmus 已从⾄少两家国际评级机构获得 BBB 投资级评级。摩根⼠丹利主导此次

融资， Firmus ⽬前正邀请其他银⾏加⼊承销团。
融资环境⽅⾯， AI 板块估值担忧持续发酵，全球借贷成本仍处⾼位，投资者对 AI 基础设施项⽬的
⻛险敞⼝更加审慎。 Firmus 回应媒体时表⽰： “Firmus 不对市场猜测发表评论，融资安排及任何
细节将在适当时候予以公告。 ”

## Document 375: Lean社区通过“形式化验证”：相信Anthropic数学家对黎曼猜想的证明是正确的

Source PDF: all report pdf/Lean社区通过“形式化验证”：相信Anthropic数学家对黎曼猜想的证明是正确的.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

传统数学突破靠同⾏逐⼀审阅论⽂、判断每⼀步推理是否成⽴。但⾯对超过 25 万⻚、短期
内⽆⼈能通读的证明草稿，形式化验证软件 Lean 成了数学界最快的 “ 公证处 ” 。
摘要：
11 ⽉ 21 ⽇，开源形式化验证社区 Lean 与 Mathlib 发布联合声明，确认 Anthropic 数学家 Levent
Alpöge 对黎曼猜想的证明成⽴。两家机构联合背书称：
11 ⽉ 20 ⽇，即 Anthropic 上市次⽇， Alpöge 宣布与 AI 模型 Claude 合作找到了黎曼猜想及⼴义黎曼
猜想的证明⽅法，并公开逾 1700 万⾏ Lean 源码。因超过 25 万⻚的⽂字版证明难以阅读⽽未公
开，加之宣布时点紧贴 IPO ，外界⼀度对其真伪与商业动机存疑，预测平台上的相关赔率也反映
出市场分歧。
24 ⼩时后， Lean 社区⽤四重形式化核验给出了计算机级别的回应。但机器验证与⼈类读懂证明
是两码事 —— 验证链上甚⾄出现了 Claude 编写的内核，部分收尾测试尚未结束，要想克雷数学

研究所的 100 万美元⼤奖也还需要经过⾄少两年的公开检验。
24 ⼩时核完 1700 万⾏代码： Lean 如何验真，边界⼜在哪？
传统数学突破靠同⾏逐⼀审阅论⽂、判断每⼀步推理是否成⽴。但⾯对超过 25 万⻚、短期内⽆
⼈能通读的证明草稿，形式化验证软件 Lean 成了数学界最快的 “ 公证处 ” 。
Lean 由巴西计算机科学家 Leonardo de Moura 于 2013 年创⽴，最初在微软研究院开发，后在亚
⻢逊云科技 AWS 延续推进， 2021 年推出重写版 Lean 4 ，现由 Lean FRO ⽀持。在这种编程语⾔
和证明助⼿中，数学家须把每个定义、前置结论和推理步骤写成计算机可读代码，由程序逐⼀
校验逻辑是否严密。
“ 完全有信⼼ Alpöge 对黎曼猜想的证明是正确的 ” 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

其始于 2017 年的社区共享数学库 Mathlib 规模已超 100 万⾏，⽂档中甚⾄给黎曼猜想留了⼀句注
脚，称构造其证明 “ 值⼀百万美元 ” 。 Lean 曾于 2021 年验证 Peter Scholze 的凝聚数学证明， 2023
年协助陶哲轩形式化多项式 Freiman-Ruzsa 猜想证明，也是 Google DeepMind 打造 AlphaProof
的底层⼯具，并被⽤于亚⻢逊 Cedar 策略语⾔及 Rust 代码验证。
但代码能跑通编译，并不等于黎曼猜想已被攻克。数学家 Kit Yates 在 Substack 专栏中指出，
Lean 校验虽远⽐让另⼀个 AI 评判严谨，专家仍须额外确认三点：代码证明的确实是黎曼猜想⽽
⾮更弱命题、定义忠实还原了 ζ 函数等标准对象、推理中未夹带额外假设。 Lean 语⾔参考⼿册也
警⽰，系统⽆法检查新公理是否⾃洽，更须警惕能 “ 证明⼀切 ” 的占位符sorry。
此次 Lean 社区敢于在⼀天内拍板，是因为 Alpöge 的代码库全部通过了伦敦帝国理⼯学院数学家

Kevin Buzzard 在 11 ⽉ 12 ⽇提出的四项硬标准。
第 ⼀ 关 是 核 对 “ 题 ⼲ ” 。 该 代 码 库 仅 直 接 依 赖 M a t h l i b ， 所 证 定 理 指 向 M a t h l i b 内 置 的
RiemannHypothesis标准定义 —— 在所有使 ζ 函数为零的复数中，除 -2 、 -4 、 -6 等平凡零点
外，实部均为 1/2 。该定义早已经过 Lean 和数论专家反复核验。
第⼆关是独⽴复现。这份拆为 14 个压缩⽂件、涵盖 175,187 个模块的代码库对算⼒要求极⾼，单
⼈完整验证约需 100GB 内存与 70GB 硬盘。⿇省理⼯学院数学家 Andrew Sutherland 使⽤ Lean 与
Mathlib v4.33.1 独⽴完成⽆错编译，且 Mathlib ⽂件与官⽅缓存⼀致。联合声明显⽰，已有数⼗
⼈复现编译，并⽣成完全⼀致的原始证明导出⽂件，体量达 61.6GB 。
第三关有关 “ 暗箱操作 ” 。社区另建仅导⼊ Mathlib 的隔离⽂件，动⽤comparator与con-leche

--compare两套⼯具⽐对，双双确认 Alpöge 所证命题与 Mathlib 原定义完全吻合。在公理审查
上， Sutherland 对主⽂件Main.lean中的五个定理运⾏#print axioms指令，结果显⽰它们
仅 依 赖 Lean 七 条 标 准 公 理 中 的 三 条 核 ⼼ 公 理 ——propext 、Classical.choice 与
Quot.sound ， 不 含 任 何sorryAx ， 且 全 部 源 ⾃ 核 ⼼ 库 。 “ 这 ⾥ ⾯ 有 猫 腻 的 概 率 近 似 为
零， ”Sutherland 对《科学美国⼈》说。
最后⼀关针对验证软件本⾝。 Lean 官⽅内核历史上曾出现可靠性漏洞，发现后 4 ⼩时即被修复，
数学家 Elliot Glazer 也将 Lean ⾃⾝存在漏洞的概率估计在 1% 以下。为隔绝源码潜在恶意代码、
规避单⼀内核缺陷，社区跳过源码，直接将 61.6GB 的惰性导出⽂件交由多语⾔独⽴内核交叉检
验。
除 Lean 官⽅内核及全量放⾏ 175,187 个模块的leanchecker、lean4lean外， Rust 编写的第

三⽅内核nanoda耗时 27 分 42 秒接受完整导出；con-leche仅⽤ 3 分 15 秒审完 146.8 万条声明，
⽆⼀拒绝；con-ron同样过关。con-leche与con-ron本⾝还附带相对于集合论的形式化⾃洽
证明，从数学逻辑上确保其⽆法接受错误证明。
验证通过的效⼒已体现在具体数学推论上。结合已有形式化成果， Sutherland ⻚⾯确认已从该证
明中推导出关于整数因数之和的 Robin 不等式与 Lagarias 不等式，同样只依赖三条标准公理。
1984 年，法国数学家 Guy Robin 证明，对任何⼤于 5040 的整数 n ，其所有因数之和⼩于  这⼀直
观命题与黎曼猜想完全等价 —— 其中 γ 为欧拉常数， e^γ 约等于 1.78 。
不过，对照 Lean 联合声明与 Sutherland 的 MIT 验证⻚，这场验证仍有⼏处尚未彻底收⼝的技术边
界。
Lean 联合声明虽宣布comparator和官⽅内核均已通过，但未公布具体⼯具版本与详细输出⽇

志。截⾄ 11 ⽉ 21 ⽇ 12:55 UTC ， Sutherland 本⼈在 Lean v4.36.0 上的端到端comparator运⾏已
完成命题⽐对与公理检查，内核阶段仍在继续， Lean 默认与严格内核也尚未出结果；他此前的

### Page 3

构建与导出未在沙盒中完成，尚待与沙盒构建结果⽐对。⾄于 Lean v4.36.0 捆绑的旧版con-
leche和con-ron因不⽀持证明中的⼀种类型返回退出码 2 ， Sutherland ⻚⾯注明这并不意味着
证明被拒绝。
在证明范围上也存在⼝径差。 Elliot Glazer 于 11 ⽉ 20 ⽇称形式化命题实为⼴义黎曼猜想，
Sutherland ⻚⾯则更为审慎：主定理是 Mathlib 定义的黎曼猜想，其余四个定理针对狄利克雷 L 函
数，⻚⾯仅在此限定意义上提及 “GRH” 。 Sutherland 同时提醒，即便公理检查⽆误，最终结论仍
以 Mathlib 定义忠于数学本意、验证内核⾃⾝可靠为前提。
从证明到 “ 判卷 ”
把视⻆拉向幕后， Claude 的⾝影贯穿了从提出证明到参与 “ 判卷 ” 的多个环节。
按 Alpöge 附上的研究时间线，这项⼯作始于 10 ⽉ 6 ⽇的 “Stepanov ⾏列式 ” ，⾄当地时间 11 ⽉ 15 ⽇
深夜⾄ 16 ⽇凌晨告成， 11 ⽉ 16 ⽇的记录上仅剩 “GRH” 三个字⺟。该时间线据称由 Claude 整理。

Alpöge 表⽰，⾃⼰负责指明⽅向， Claude 随后提出突破性步骤 —— ⼀条⻓度约等于所界定素数
和平⽅根的 “theta 提升 ” 链，每次提升对应经典函数域论证所依赖、在整数域上却极难找到的 “ 重
数 ” 。 “ 在接近终点时， Claude 远远超过了我。不， Claude 简直是在终点附近⻜翔、跳舞、闪耀， ”
他在声明中写道。
到了核验阶段， Claude 依然在场。 Sutherland 验证⻚开头署名显⽰，整套检查通过 Claude
Code 运⾏；⽽ 3 分 15 秒审完 146.8 万条声明的con-leche，据 Lean 创始⼈ Leonardo de Moura
上⽉在博客中介绍，是⾸个⽤ Lean 写成并被证明⾃洽的内核 —— 代码与⾃洽证明均由 Claude 完
成，由 Lean FRO 的 Joachim Breitner 严格把关。因为有形式化⾃洽证明兜底，且最终结论与
Rust 内核nanoda等多个第三⽅独⽴内核⼀致，验证信⽤并不取决于单⼀⼯具。

这种推进速度超出了 Anthropic 三个⽉前的⾃⾝预期。今年 8 ⽉，公司曾公布未公开版 Claude 配
合 Lean 的研究成果，将关键线上零点⽐例下界从 41.6% 推⾼⾄ 67.2% ，并通过了comparator
检验。当时 Anthropic 明确表态，并不预期这些技术能最终证明黎曼猜想。
仅仅三个⽉后就实现跨越，却恰好撞上 11 ⽉ 19 ⽇ IPO 后的敏感窗⼝。 Alpöge 在声明中主动承认
“ 精确的时机⾮常糟糕 ” ，透露完成后曾询问 Anthropic ⾸席执⾏官 Dario Amodei 能否公开， “ 最终
我们弄清楚了，确实存在需要遵守的限制 ” 。他否认外界对发布时机的负⾯猜测，称这种 “ 明显的
愤世嫉俗的看法 ” 是错误的，并坦⾔⾃⼰ “ 实在⽆法再等下去了 ” ，愿承担全部责任。
舆论狂欢与学界克制
北京时间 11 ⽉ 21 ⽇ 23:12 ， Lean 官⽅公告在社交平台 X 发布，迅速激起科技圈与主流学界的讨
论。
AI 圈情绪迅速沸腾： AI 研究者姚顺⾬早在 20 ⽇晚便发帖惊呼 “OMG, Riemann Hypothsis?!” ；官

⽅声明出炉后， AI 资讯作者 Rohan Paul 概括称 “167 年后，黎曼猜想终于被 AI 完成了 ” ，评论账号
Dr Singularity 则感叹 “What a time to be alive” 。⽹友还翻出 OpenAI ⾸席执⾏官 Sam Altman 于
2022 年在 X 上半开玩笑追问 “ 黎曼猜想什么时候能搞定 ” 的旧帖，调侃 “Anthropic 抢在你前⾯做到
了 ” 。

### Page 4

主流学者的措辞则明显更紧。 11 ⽉ 21 ⽇早间，在 Lean 官⽅联合声明尚未发布前，哥伦⽐亚⼤学
数学与物理教授 Brian Greene 发帖介绍 AI“ 可能攻克了凌驾于所有难题之上的黎曼猜想 ” ，但⽤词
保留余地，并⾮其本⼈独⽴核验。罗格斯⼤学数学系主任 Alex Kontorovich 虽对证明成功愈发乐
观，当⽇在 X 上的动作也仅是转发《 Quanta Magazine 》关于 Alpöge 声明的报道。

3 条评论
在功劳归属上，学界同样拒绝将此简化为 “AI 独⼒破题 ” 。普林斯顿⼤学⼀位数学与 AI 研究⼈员强
调，⼈类参与仍是利⽤⼤语⾔模型解决数学问题的最佳⽅式， Alpöge 应当获得与 Claude 同等的
认可。 Sutherland 在独⽴编译通过后，也称这是⼀场 “ ⼈类的胜利 ” 。
更棘⼿的现实在于，机器已经验证，⼈类却还读不懂。曼彻斯特⼤学数学家 Thomas Bloom 在 X
上直⾔，他分不清更令⾃⼰震惊的是 AI 声称证明了黎曼猜想，还是 AI ⽣成了⼀份转化为⼈类可读
⽂本后超过 25 万⻚、且逻辑连贯的证明。
2006 年菲尔兹奖得主陶哲轩在个⼈博客中给出初步判断：这份证明似乎综合运⽤了朗兰兹纲领
和算术⼏何的现代研究成果，验证与消化仍需时间， “ 好在这⼀次，⾄少还有⼀位⼈类作者可以

沟通 ” 。
接下来，拆解与简化这份庞⼤证明将成为数学界的核⼼课题。 Lean 与 Mathlib 社区在声明末尾表
⽰，期待数学界接下来的⼯作能够阐释该证明中的新思想。 Alpöge 本⼈也预计，这份体量惊⼈
的证明 “ 很可能会被⼤幅简化，⽽且可能很快就能实现 ” 。
即便计算机已经给出了肯定答复，数学界的正式加冕仍须跨越漫⻓的制度⻔槛。按克雷数学研
究所规则，千禧年⼤奖难题的 100 万美元奖⾦要求解答必须正式发表、经过⾄少两年的公开检
验，并获得国际数学界普遍认可。
对 Alpöge ⽽⾔，他⼝中这个 “ 科学领域某种更伟⼤事物的⼀个黎明 ” ，距离真正尘埃落定还有数年
时间。

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
•  Momentum in US share markets quickly faded after a strong
open, leaving the S&P 500 (–1.9%), Dow Jones (–2.1%) and
NASDAQ (–2.2%) all lower. Anthropic’s IPO was the focal
point for broader sentiment, having rallied to $115 shortly

after the open, before retreating sharply back to $96, still
above the $83 IPO price. The shakiness around the AI trade
was compounded by reports of a cyber-security attack
impacting JP Morgan, Visa and Mastercard, adding to the
string of recent digital issues among major US corporates.
•  Stocks were also weaker across the pond, with the Euro
Stoxx 50 (–0.7%) and London’s FTSE 100 (–0.9%) both down
on the day. Meanwhile, worries around the AI trade continued

to weigh heavily on Hong Kong, where stocks posted their
third consecutive daily decline (–1.9%). Both Tokyo (+0.7%)
and Seoul (+1.4%) managed to hold onto recent momentum
though. The ASX 200 dropped 1.0% yesterday, and futures
markets are pointing to another weak open.
•  Treasuries benefitted from the more volatile risk environment,
seeing the 2Y fall 4bps to 4.77% while the 10Y edged 2bps
lower to 5.04%. Market pricing for the Fed hasn’t shift much

so far this week – with hikes fully priced in for Dec-26, Mar-27
and Jul-27, the hawkish outlook for the Fed is setting up the
longest run for the 10Y above 5% since 2007. Yields were
mixed elsewhere across the globe: 10Y Bunds and Gilts up
4bps and 3bps respectively, while the 10Y JGB fell 3bps.
• Locally, the October Labour Force Survey surprised materially
to the downside, with a sharp fall in employment and the
unemployment rate rising to 4.9%. That drove a swift

repricing for the RBA, with swaps markets now definitively
pricing in an RBA rate cut during the second half of 2027,
materially more dovish than the ‘no hikes, no cuts’ pricing
prior. ACGBs rallied in response, with the 3Y and 10Y falling
16bps and 12bps to 4.57% and 4.96% respectively, bringing
yields to its lowest level since August.
•  The USD received a solid bid while share markets were
contending with the sudden drop, seeing the DXY retrace

a fall earlier in the session to finish broadly steady near its
cycle high at 103.9. Moves were fairly modest across most
DXY pairs, with declines in the Sterling and Yen the only
notable moves. However, the Aussie dollar sold-off much
more sharply given the weaker labour market data, seeing it
finish 0.8% lower on the day; at around USD0.666, it is at its
weakest level so far this year.
•  Oil prices rose edged only slightly higher overnight, with

Brent up 0.3% to US$92.2/bbl. Skirmishes between the
US and Iran have been increasing in recent days, adding
to fears of a more prolonged escalatory phase. However,
President Trump recently stated that he is “not rushing into
any decision on Iran”, and that he will see what happens after
the beginning of the year. Both copper and aluminium moved
higher, up 0.4% and 1.7% respectively, only partly recovering
from the sharp sell-off over the past week or so. Gold prices

also fell 1.5% to around the US$3,670/oz mark.
International Data
In the US, initial jobless claims were essentially unchanged last
week at 203k, consistent with little-to-no active job shedding.
The leading index edged down from 0.0% in September to
–0.3% in October, pointing to a deceleration in activity growth
over the coming 6 months. The Philadelphia Fed business
outlook declined from 20.9 to 6.3 in November, against an
expectation for little-to-no change in the month. The Kansas

City Fed manufacturing index meanwhile rose from 11 to 18.
Businesses across the US economy face disparate and volatile
conditions at present.
Local Data
In Australia, the October Labour Force Survey surprised
materially to the downside, with employment declining by
43.5k, the unemployment rate jumped to 4.9%. Slower hiring,
alongside signs of a pick-up in firing, suggest there is more
weakening in the labour market to come (see here).

## Document 394: OpenAI爆改旗舰GPU，AMD直接送厂流片！

Source PDF: all report pdf/OpenAI爆改旗舰GPU，AMD直接送厂流片！.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

OpenAI 利⽤ GPT 重写 AMD 旗舰 GPU 电路代码，不到 5 个⽉实现最⾼ 14% 的 PPA 提升，部分
设计已送⼚流⽚。此前， GPT 已参与⾃研芯⽚设计并通过硅⽚验证。下⼀步， OpenAI 将联
合 Synopsys 训练新模型，让 AI 直接调⽤ EDA ⼯具，进⼀步提升芯⽚设计效率。
摘要：
AI 这把⽕，彻底烧进芯⽚圈了。
OpenAI 刚刚官宣，⾃家 GPT 给 AMD 旗舰 GPU 改电路，不到 5 个⽉，核⼼指标 PPA 最⾼提升
14% 。
要知道，这套设计 AMD ⼯程师，可是优化了好⼏年。
算下来， OpenAI ⼀上⼿直接把⼀年的进度条，暴⼒压缩到 5 个⽉。
AI 重写的电路，已经被 AMD 送去流⽚了。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

OpenAI 榨⼲芯⽚设计  5 个⽉再榨 14%
⼀块被顶级团队反复优化过的旗舰 GPU ，再往上挤⼀点都难。
⽤官博的原话说，这颗 AMD 旗舰芯⽚是「被⾼度优化过的设计」。
这⼀次， OpenAI 所做的，就是重写描述芯⽚电路的 SystemVerilog RTL 的代码。
从计算、控制、到数据搬运，所有核⼼电路，全在它的修改范围内。
⽽且，每改⼀处，它就调 Synopsys 的⼯具验⼀遍 ——
改完的模块⾏为跟原来⼀样， PPA （功耗、性能、⾯积）三头怎么取舍。

### Page 3

就这样，不到 5 个⽉的时间， AI 在 AMD 这套千锤百炼的旗舰 GPU IP 基线上，最⾼抠出了 14% 的
PPA 净提升！
OpenAI 是这么评价的， AI 在 RTL 层⾯的推理能⼒，已经达到了「专家级微架构师」的⽔平。
今天，总裁 Greg 在 DealBook 峰会上提到了这项成果，直⾔「我们已构建出更强⼤的 AI ，并取得
了巨⼤的经济价值」。
值得⼀提的是， AI 改出来的代码，已经在 AMD 内部⽤上了。
⼀部分放进最近送去制造的那版设计，另⼀部分写进⽣产设计⽂件，留给以后的版本。
就连 CPU 也有份， AMD ⾃⼰验过，⽤模型写的设计，下⼀代旗舰 CPU ⼏个关键模块的 PPA 提了
个位数百分⽐。
对此， AMD 掌⻔⼈苏妈亲⾃转发：「为这些了不起的成果骄傲」。

### Page 4

GPT-4o 魔改⾃研「辣椒」跑通验证了
这套打法， OpenAI 先在⾃家芯⽚  Jalapeño （辣椒）上⽤过。
⼀个乘法器，模型重新设计以后，综合出来的⾯积⼩了约 15% ，⼀次运算从 2 个时钟周期压到 1
个。
另⼀个加法器，⾯积也⼩了约 15% ，延迟还是 2 个周期。
整个算下来，向量单元的标准单元⾯积省了约 13% ，矩阵单元省了约 10% 。

### Page 5

这些不是仿真⾥的数，辣椒的硅⽚点亮时都验过了。
辣椒是 OpenAI 跟博通做的第⼀款⾃研推理芯⽚， 2025 年初团队就拿 GPT-4o 参与辅助设计。
新⼀代 GPT 越来越会造芯
不论是 AMD 旗舰 GPU ，还是辣椒，都是 OpenAI ⽤ AI 设计芯⽚的亮眼成绩单。
那么，「 GPT 全系家族」整体情况⼜如何呢？
OpenAI 专⻔搭了⼀套评测体系，挑了两款开源的 32 位 RISC-V 处理器核 ——PicoRV32 和 Ibex 。
主要考核两⼤任务。

### Page 6

先给到现成的 RTL 代码，让 GPT 优化现有设计，提升吞吐量。
其次，只给书⾯需求和接⼝模板，⾄于数据通路和流⽔线怎么切分，全由 AI ⾃⼰拍板。
最终，在每次约 11.2 万 token 的相近预算下，GPT-6.2 Astra 在两个内核上平均分都最⾼。
在优化 PicoRV32 这项上，它的得分约是 GPT-6 Astra 的两倍。
有意思的是，同等预算下，五款模型从头写的分数都⽐改现成的⾼。
毕竟，从 0 设计的⾃由度更⾼， GPT 顺⼿就把原核⾥⼀些⽤处不⼤的东西砍掉了，⽐如性能计数
器、额外指令
反正它们对跑分吞吐也没啥贡献，⾯积上限也放到了原设计的 16 倍。

## Document 439: US MARKETS CALL_ A Bull-Market Correction Or A Tech-Wreck Bear Market_ Also_ Our New S&P 500 Targets.

Source PDF: all report pdf/US MARKETS CALL_ A Bull-Market Correction Or A Tech-Wreck Bear Market_ Also_ Our New S&P 500 Targets..pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-22
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US MARKETS CALL: A Bull-Market Correction Or A Tech-Wreck Bear Market? Also: Our New S&P 500 Targets.
Date:    9 hours ago
US MARKETS CALL: A Bull-Market Correction Or A Tech-Wreck Bear Market? Also: Our New S&P 500 Targets.

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US MARKETS CALL: A Bull-
Market Correction Or A Tech-
Wreck Bear Market? Also: Our
New S&P 500 Targets.
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 21 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Anthony Maw / Unsplash

I. Another Correction In A Bull Market
On September 15, we lowered our year-end S&P 500 target from 8,400 to
7,900. We turned more cautious on the stock market and expected buying
opportunities after the midterm elections. We got more of a buying
opportunity than we bargained for, as the S&P 500 dropped from 7,585.73
on September 15 to 6,910.39 on Friday. It is down 11.6% since its October
6 record high of 7818.93 (chart). We expected a pullback. It has turned
into a correction. We don't think it will lead to a bear market, simply
because we don't see an impending recession.

### Page 2

The Nasdaq-100 has also corrected by 19.8% since October 5 (chart). So
it is on the edge of turning into a bear market. The S&P 500 and Nasdaq-
100 both fell below their 200-day moving averages last week. Their
comparable equal-weight indexes found support at their 200-day moving
averages, so far.
The S&P 500's correction has been led by a sharp drop in its forward P/E
(charts). It plunged to 16.4 on Friday to the lowest level since the bear
market of 2022. Much of the recent damage has been attributable to the

S&P 500 Information Technology sector, particularly the S&P 500
Semiconductors industry, where the forward P/E fell to 9.4 on Friday.
Semiconductors were hit by news last week that the US government was
tightening export controls on semiconductors in response to allegations
that Nvidia and other US chip companies were indirectly selling their AI
chips to China or providing chips to other Asian data centers that the
Chinese accessed remotely. The fear is that tighter export controls will

lower the demand for chips and depress their prices. If so, then the drop in
the industry's valuation multiple will soon be followed by a slowdown in
earnings growth or even an outright drop in earnings.

### Page 3

To project the outlook for the S&P 500 in any year, we have been
multiplying our year-ahead S&P 500 EPS forecasts by a forward P/E
range of 17.0-20.0 (charts). The forward P/E and the index fell below the
bottom of our 2026 range last week. We haven't given up on a Santa
Claus rally, but are lowering our year-end S&P 500 target to 7,500. That
would be up 9.6% for the year from 6,845.50 at the end of last year.
Our S&P 500 range for next year is 7,650 to 9,000 (chart). We expect the

index to end 2027 between 8,500 and 9,000. We are sticking with our
Roaring 2020s forecast of 10,000 by 2029.

### Page 4

Maybe Elon can figure out how to buy Nvidia and hire Jensen to run it.
Nvidia is selling at just 8.1 forward earnings, as though it is still just a
gaming company (chart). Then again, Elon would have to find $3.02 trillion
to do the deal. That would be as crazy as putting people on Mars.
II. Performance
The latest Tech Wreck has been led by the semiconductor industry (chart).
It has also weighed on the MAGS ETF, which is now down 6.5% ytd
(chart). The XMAGS ETF is up by 5.4%. The S&P 500 is up just 0.9%.

### Page 5

Momentum ETFs have also been hard hit (chart).
III. Earnings
Q3's earnings data are now available for the S&P 500 (chart). Earnings
per share edged down from Q2's record results, which were boosted by
substantial mark-to-market investment gains for Alphabet and Amazon.
Revenue per share rose to a record high. The profit margin edged down to
16.3% from Q2's record high.
The industry analysts' consensus earnings estimates for 2027 and 2028
rose to record highs of $427.60 and $498.44 per share last week (chart).

### Page 6

Forward earnings rose to a record high of $421.84. It will converge with
the 2027 estimate at the end of this year. The forward P/E at the end of
last week was 16.4.
The Q3 growth rate of EPS was 27.1%, while Q4 is expected to be up
30.0% (chart).
IV. Valuation
The S&P 500 Growth forward P/E has plunged from 29.7 late last year to
17.8 now (chart). The S&P 500 Value valuation multiple has held up better
this year and is 17.3 currently.

### Page 7

The ratio of Growth's forward P/E to Value's is down to 1.03, the lowest
since the recession during the Great Financial Crisis (chart). It last peaked
in July 2024 at 1.84. The unwinding of the AI trade this year has
eliminated Growth's valuation premium over Value. That premium should
increase again once the AI selling panic subsides.
V. Sentiment
Contrarians can take some comfort from the spikes in the S&P 500 VIX
and the Nasdaq VIX to 35.9 and 45.3 on Friday (charts). With no

recession in sight, we doubt that these "fear gauges" will rise much from
here.

### Page 8

The two Bull/Bear Ratios we track are well below their long-term averages
(chart). We expect them to be even lower over the coming week. These
are also useful contrarian indicators.
V. Credit
The 10-year US Treasury bond yield fell to 5.04% last week in response to
the stock market rout (chart).
Interestingly, forward inflation-linked swaps have been mostly declining in
recent days as the stock market has weakened (chart).

### Page 9

clause.

## Document 451: When tokenization meets securitized debt markets (2026-11-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/When tokenization meets securitized debt markets (2026-11-17).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank Research Institute
Deutsche Bank
Research

### Page 2

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
When tokenization meets securitized debt markets
Asset tokenization continues to grow in 2026. Excluding stablecoins, the
market grew from ~$10bn in Jan 2025 to ~$40bn in Nov 2026. Including
tokens confined to closed or permissioned environments, the total reached
~$420bn (here).
This chartbook explores tokenization’s potential in securitized markets, where

complex structuring, long settlement times and high transaction costs make
the asset class a strong candidate for tokenization.
Today, tokenization mainly improves securities-market infrastructure by
streamlining intermediation, cutting loan origination costs and speeding up
institutional securitization. US-based companies Figure Technologies,
Centrifuge and Maple Finance illustrate the benefits.
The transformative end state combines tokenized infrastructure (represented

tokenization) with tokens transferable outside the issuing platform (distributed
tokenization). Only when both are combined could tokenization reduce credit
risk, deepen the investor base, speed up the flow of capital, and lead to a more
robust, liquid US securitized market.
In this chartbook, we: (i) explain securitization and its challenges; (ii) examine
tokenization’s potential benefits; and (iii) outline the steps needed for
widespread adoption.

….but only ~$8bn in distributed value.
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Data as of Nov 12, 2026. Credit combines asset-backed credit,
corporate credit, diversified credit and specialty finance. “Represented” refers to tokens using the blockchain
as a recordkeeping layer, enhancing transparency, reconciliation, and operational efficiency without enabling
onchain investor transfer or distribution.
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Data as of Nov 13, 2026. “Distributed”

refers to tokens transferable outside the issuing platform and between wallets; it does
not measure trading activity.
Tokenized credit’s total value is ~$50bn in November, with Figure’s HELOC
token representing $26trn…

### Page 3

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
A ~$15trn market financing housing, consumers and companies
❑ The securitized (structured credit) market includes residential real estate
(RMBS), commercial real estate (CMBS), consumer borrowing (ABS –
autos, credit cards, student loans), and corporate debt (CLOs).
❑ It is a material part of the US growth engine: ~$15trn of debt outstanding,
roughly half of 2025 US nominal GDP. About 75% is linked to US housing

market.
❑ This market emerged in the late 1970s, when the US economy struggled
with sluggish growth and high inflation from elevated energy costs.
❑ Securitization was seen as financial innovation: It let bank lenders move
debt off their balance sheets and syndicate it to public markets as a
pooled asset.
❑ In the three decades before the GFC, the market’s growth increased
capital velocity in US debt markets while simultaneously reducing
financing costs for both borrowers and originators.

❑ Securitization produces pricing efficiency by bundling a diversified pool
of assets with consistent cashflows (e.g., such as corporate loans or
monthly mortgage payments) into a security held by a bankruptcy remote
special purpose vehicle (SPV).
❑ These pooled assets are then sliced into “tranches” with varying credit
risk and corresponding levels of yield, sold to institutional investors (life
insurers to hedge funds) that find relative value in the structure and

diversification.
Residential mortgages (RMBS) plays an outsized role in the market
The US Securitized market is approaching $16trn in market size
Sources: SIFMA, Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research.
Sources: SIFMA, US Federal Reserve, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 4

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Securitization is held back by complexity, slow settlement and high costs
A CLO investor can expect their first coupon payment typically 6 months
after a deal’s pricing date
Although securitization is deeply embedded into the US financial
system, several challenges remain:
❑ Inherent complexity: Collateralization complicates ownership
tracking, especially as deals are upsized, widely distributed and

traded in a secondary market. This was visible during the GFC, and in
recent pockets of credit stress – the tracking of collateral and of
asset ownership can present logistical hurdles over the lifetime of an
asset.
❑ Long settlement times: Complex structuring can delay settlement
and prolong the liquidity gap between allocating capital to a deal and
when the first coupon distribution takes place.  For example, a new
issue CLO can take up to two months from pricing to close, with an

additional four months before the first coupon distribution date takes
place.
❑ High transaction costs: Intermediaries between the buyer and seller
provide essential services but increase costs when issuing a deal.
Additionally, the ongoing deal administration from monthly collateral
monitoring to coupon distributions adds an additional layer of
logistical complexity to a securitization post close. The key
stakeholders required in this value chain range from arranging banks,

trustees, rating agencies, and lawyers.
Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research.
4

### Page 5

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
❑ Tokenization records assets or claims as digital tokens on a distributed
ledger (i.e., blockchain), using public or permissioned networks.
❑ Potential benefits: Faster transfers, automated servicing and smaller
investment units.
❑ Shared records can reduce reconciliation, but trading hours, redemption
terms and investor access remain product-specific.
Two issuance models:

❑ On-chain (native): The security or fund share is issued directly on the
distributed ledger/blockchain.
❑ Off-chain (tokenized representation): The underlying asset remains
outside the blockchain, but ownership claims and economic rights are
represented digitally on-chain.
❑ Tokens may be then either transferred to external wallets (distributed) or
platform-bound on the blockchain (represented).
Tokenization can move issuance, settlement and servicing onto a shared ledger

### Page 6

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Credit is tokenized at wrapper or asset level; most value is still represented
There are two broad approaches to tokenizing credit
❑ Wrapper-level tokenization: The underlying credit assets remain in a
conventional portfolio structure, while a fund or SPV is tokenized, e.g.
Galaxy’s new-issue CLO with debt tranches issued and tokenized on
Avalanche, and Apollo Diversified Credit (ACRED).

❑ Asset-level / native tokenization: Blockchain is integrated into the
underlying credit asset's issuance, ownership record and/or lifecycle.
Examples are Figure Technologies and Tradable, which use blockchains to
support loan administration, ownership records, payments tracking, and
servicing.
❑ Figure can be broken down into distributed vs represented, given it
can be reclassified if they become movable and transferrable.
❑ Asset-level vs wrapper-level shows what is tokenized; distributed vs

represented shows how the token can circulate.
State of tokenized securities
❑ Private credit stands ~$50bn in total (~$8bn distributed vs ~$42bn
represented). Includes asset-backed credit ($33bn), specialty finance
($13bn), corporate credit ($2bn), and diversified credit ($795mn).
❑ Use case is mostly record-keeping; growing adoption for transferable
tokenized credit funds.
❑ Tokenized securitized market is also still mostly at the fund level (i.e. KKR &

Securitize, Hamilton Lane) but efforts to tokenize underlying assets is
growing (i.e. Figure, Centrifuge, Intain). E.g. Figure Technologies is
reportedly prepping its first fully tokenized RMBS issuance in the US for
late 2026/early 2027.

### Page 7

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Benefits: Transparency – one shared record replaces three reconciled copies
❑ Problem: Traditional securitization can be opaque. Investors often rely on
periodic, manually reconciled reports rather than direct visibility.
❑ Solution: A shared ledger for every party, tokenization allows investors to
access an audit trail time-stamped with evidence of tamper history, visible

holder register to both issuer and regulator, and loan level by default,
making it permissioned.
Potential use case examples
❑ Immutable, public records of the blockchain prevents double pledging of
assets (e.g., First Brands controversy).
❑ Illuminates circular financing (e.g., AI data centre capex buildout), where a
company provides liquidity to a second company so that it can buy its
goods/services.
❑ Commingled cash flows can obscure a company’s operating

performance and can make it more complex for an investor in
evaluating its underlying economic health and cash position.
❑ Enables more bespoke structures (i.e., multi-strategy CDOs for private
credit loan books), by mapping investor exposure to underlying assets,
helping establish correlations and credit risk that may not be immediately
apparent.

### Page 8

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Benefits: Liquidity – platforms can build a supply-and-demand flywheel
❑ Problem: The structural complexity of the asset class can negatively
impact investor sentiment, serving as a headwind for market growth, and
ultimately impact market depth, liquidity, and prices. And the operational
complexity required for bringing a securitized deal to market can lead to

long settlement times, producing a liquidity gap for an investor.
❑ Solution: The asset transparency resulting from the use of a shared
ledger as core, underlying market infrastructure would help to draw-in
more investors that have traditionally sat on the sidelines. This has been a
hurdle for both structured credit and private credit assets.
❑ A decrease in the perceived complexity cost should naturally lead to a
cohort of net-new investors allocating to the asset class, enabling both

market growth and the added liquidity achieved from a developed
secondary market. An analog, TradFi example of this can be found in the
CLO market, where primary market growth has enabled a robust
secondary market to evolve, further supporting investor sentiment for the
asset class.  The scale achieved has been a factor in driving spreads
tighter over the last few years.
❑ The process simplification enabled by using a block-chain as
foundational infrastructure can lead to time savings, spurring demand

which then attracts greater supply.  Figure Technology’s “Connect”
Platform, a two-sided marketplace developed on the Provenance
blockchain, is an example, connecting loan originators with asset buyers.
Its HELOC offering, which lowers the time and cost required for an
originator to price a loan, has been the service which has played an
outsized role in driving market adoption – Q3 saw growth, with Figure
Connect volume reaching $4.3bn compared to Q2’s $2.8bn.

Connect is an early example of a tokenized securitization marketplace
Sources: Figure Technology Solutions financial filings, AlphaSense,, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 9

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Benefits: Lower costs and faster settlement – ~50–90 bps to bring a deal to market
❑ Problem: Intermediaries (e.g., arranging banks, rating agencies,
lawyers, and trustees) provide essential services for deal activity and
the ongoing administration of cash distributions and collateral
monitoring.
❑ Yet, this costs investors and borrowers fees and processing time,

delaying when an investor receives their first cash distribution by up to
4 months.
❑ Solution: Embedding blockchain into the securitization value chain
could substitute outright (the rails and software providing the service)
or help stakeholders coordinate more efficiently, lowering transaction
costs and time to value for investors. This would also compress
settlement timelines and speed up capital flows.
❑ Net impact: As a distributed ledger, blockchain has features relevant to

securitization: immutable records ensure consistent, reliable data
permeance, and distribution gives participants greater data access.
❑ Embedding blockchain in the securitized value chain would enable four
qualities for the market that would unlock the next stage of growth:
transparency, liquidity, cost efficiency, and speed of settlement.
❑ The savings generated could bring deal costs down materially, by
upwards of 85%.
Bringing a securitized asset to market carries with it transaction costs

### Page 10

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Securitization has supported US growth; blockchain could be the next step
The size of the US Securitized market is ~45% of US GDP❑ Structured credit has been an asset class traditionally associated with
financial innovation: it turns a pool of assets that individually carry lower
credit ratings and more credit risk, into a structured pool whose senior
tranche is often AAA.

❑ And it has been an economic growth driver – at $15trn of debt
outstanding, it is roughly 20% of US nonfinancial debt outstanding, and
just north of 45% of US GDP.
❑ The next inflection point of growth for the market could come from using
blockchain technology to optimize the securitization value chain,
embedding origination, structuring, distribution, and administration on-
chain as programmable, automated services.
❑ The continued growth of stablecoins in the years ahead, in addition to US

Treasury funds will be a necessary precursor for the growth of the
tokenized, securitized markets, as it has been for the Structured credit
markets in the TradFi world.
❑ An important relative value feature that a Structured asset provides is the
yield over US government debt – as more investment flows into the DeFi
space, Tokenized Structured products will see material growth.
❑Ultimately, the market’s expansion would be a tailwind for US GDP
growth.

Sources: SIFMA, FRED (St Louis Federal Reserve), Deutsche Bank Research.

EU
Tokenized securities fall outside MiCA. Falls entirely under EU financial

protection and trading rules).
UK
Digital sandbox, 16 firms are issuing and settling tokenized assets in the
Digital Securities sandbox. A tokenization roadmap is due later this year,
with a custody consultation in H1 2027.
Hong Kong Puts security tokens under the same regulation as traditional securities.
Singapore Project Guardian includes native issuance of asset-backed security and several tokenized private credit funds.
Japan Puts security tokens under the same regulation as traditional securities.

China Banned security tokens on shore, but off-shore tokens can have regulatory approval
❑ The tokenized RWA market remains small ($421bn in Nov 2026)
relative to traditional markets, and token issuance does not
automatically create secondary-market liquidity. Tokenized
securitized debt faces similar challenges to broader tokenized trends.
❑ Secondary-market liquidity remains low. Issuance growth has not
consistently translated into active trading or deep markets.

❑ Regulatory uncertainty: Fragmented or incomplete frameworks slow
institutional adoption and complicate integration with traditional
financial infrastructure. Legal uncertainty persists over whether token
ownership conveys ownership of the underlying asset or a claim on it.
❑ Fragmented infrastructure: Public blockchains, permissioned
institutional ledgers and hybrid platforms often lack interoperability,
fragmenting markets. Clearstream, DTCC and Euroclear published a

joint framework to address this in March 2026.
❑ Limited integration with existing custody, settlement, and record-
keeping arrangements prevents institutions from scaling tokenized
products. Proprietary infrastructure and centralized controls also
raise questions about ownership and governance.

### Page 12

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026 12
Outlook: Tokenization is still infrastructure-led; gains are operational today, credit-risk and liquidity benefits come
later
Tokenized securitization still mostly used for record-keeping purposes; how
much financial activity is adopting blockchain is still small
❑ Tokenization still mostly helps with the infrastructure/plumbing layer of the
securitized market: data, servicing, settlement and collateral mobility.

❑ Hence, distributed value of the tokenized securitized market ($8bn) remains
small compared to represented ($42bn).
❑ In contrast, US Treasury debt remains the largest (ex-stablecoins) on-chain
tokenized asset segment reaching $14bn (distributed) in November 2026.
❑ Demand reflects appetite for liquid, short-term, risk-free collateral, that can
generate yield unlike illiquid segments like private credit.
Potential to transform credit risk, but increased liquidity is further out

❑ Industry is currently in the infrastructure phase. Gains are so far operational:
lower costs, faster settlement times and data transparency.
❑ Move towards tokenizing underlying assets (rather than fund-wrapper style)
has potential to effectively reduce credit risk by countering double-pledging,
settlement risks.
❑ Tokenization remains concentrated among a small number of issuers and lacks
a deep, and liquid secondary market. Distributed value is still small, illustrating

limited activity in actual borrowing demand.
❑ Near term, tokenized Treasuries and money market funds will be the first
market to scale. Longer-term opportunities for private credit, once efforts to
integrate tokenized infrastructure with legacy systems (i.e. DTCC, NYSE,
Nasdaq), money (stablecoins, tokenized deposits), and broader regulation
progresses.

Financial Market 
▎通膨壓力續高，年底升息預期仍存 
10 月上旬以來，美債殖利率自高檔回落，高殖利率逐
步吸引買盤，股市震盪亦帶動避險資金回流債市。不
過，受燃料與能源價格上揚帶動，9 月 CPI 年增率由
3.4%升至 3.7%，PPI 年增率亦由 5.6%升至 6.3%，核
心 CPI 與核心 PPI 年增幅也高於預期，顯示物價壓力
仍高。加上零售銷售月增 0.8%優於預期、初領失業金
人數維持低位，反映消費與勞動市場仍具韌性，市場
因此雖傾向 Fed 本月先按兵不動，但 12 月再升息 1
碼的預期仍存。 
圖 1  9 月 CPI、PPI 年增率同步走高

▎避險買盤增溫，殖利率回落、債市修復 
近期美債買盤回升也反映在公債標售結果，3 年、10
年及 30 年期標售需求皆表現穩健或強勁，提供長端美
債支撐。雖然中東情勢反覆使油價維持高檔，通膨預
期仍偏高，但 Fed 官員多傾向先觀察，緩和短期升息
壓力。此外，高殖利率環境使 AI 與科技股估值面臨修
正壓力，股市震盪帶動避險資金流入美債，使 10 年期
美債殖利率自 10 月初 5.30%高位顯著回降至低點
5.04%後反彈。

◼ 9 月 CPI、PPI 升溫，消費與就業具韌性，年底升息預期仍存。不過，美債標售穩

性亦有利，技術面改善，企業獲利強勁，信用面穩健。美投等債殖利率仍處高檔，
高票息提供報酬緩衝，歷史經驗顯示，高殖利率通常伴隨較佳未來 12 個月報酬。 
◼ 產業配置上，金融債存續期間較短、利率敏感度較低，加上銀行貸款與存款維持
成長，資產品質穩健，防禦性相對較佳。科技債殖利率與 OAS 高於整體投等債市
場，反映 AI 資本支出擴張推升發債供給，但科技企業財務體質仍佳，利差走寬後
收益補償提升。整體投資可偏向中短天期，依風險承受度與收益需求擇優布局。

▎Q4 淨供給緩和，投等債利差有望收斂 
企業債發行量維持高檔，仍對債市形成一定壓力，但進
入 Q4 後，美國投等債市場的供給壓力可望逐步緩和。
若進一步觀察「淨淨」發行量（net-net issuance），
即扣除到期本金、贖回款與票息等回流資金後，市場實
際需消化的新增公司債供給，根據 J.P. Morgan 預估，
Q4「淨淨」發行量將逐月下降，新增供給對利差的壓力
可望減輕。

圖 5  Q4 美國投等債淨淨供給量預估逐月下降

資料來源：JPM，2026/10/2

同時，過去 4 年平均來看，美國投等債利差在 11 月、 
12 月偏向收斂，年底季節性有助支撐利差表現。供給
壓力下降與年底季節性相互配合，使第 4 季美國投等債
利差具備較有利的技術面條件。

差較月初收窄，正值代表利差擴大。

▎企業獲利預估強勁，支撐信用基本面 
近期殖利率上行雖受到通膨與貨幣政策預期影響，部分
也反映美國經濟與企業獲利仍具韌性。AI 基礎建設需求
與大型科技企業資本支出持續擴張，加上企業利潤率維
持高檔，成為獲利成長的重要支撐。標普 500 企業獲利
自 2026 年以來明顯加速，第 1 季與第 2 季分別年成長
29%與 52%，Q3 與 Q4 預估仍達 30%與 28%。獲利
動能延續，有助維持投等債發行公司的償債能力與財務
彈性，支撐高利率環境下的信用韌性。

圖 7 標普 500 企業 Q3、Q4 獲利年增率將維持高成長

資料來源：JPM，2026/10/2

▎高殖利率提供報酬緩衝 
目前美國投等債殖利率仍處相對高檔，較高的殖利率可
提高票息緩衝，也為後續報酬提供較有利的起點。根據
JPM 自 1990 年以來的歷史資料顯示，美國投等債當期
殖利率與未來 12 個月總報酬呈正向關係，殖利率較高
時，後續 12 個月報酬通常也較佳。在利率仍可能波動
的環境下，較高票息有助吸收部分價格波動，使目前的
收益條件較過去低殖利率時期更具支撐。 
圖 8 高殖利率通常伴隨較佳 12 個月報酬

資料來源：JPM，2026/10/12 
▎金融偏防禦，科技債補償提升 
綜合信用基本面、供需條件與收益水準，美國投等債整
體展望仍偏正向。在產業方面，金融債在殖利率接近整
體投等債市場的情況下，存續期間明顯較短。近期大型
銀行財報亦顯示，貸款與存款維持成長，整體資產品質
仍穩健，為金融債基本面提供支撐。科技債殖利率與
OAS 則高於整體市場，主要反映 AI 資本支出擴張帶動
發債供給快速增加。儘管融資需求升高，大型科技企業
整體資產負債表仍相對穩健，利差走寬後的收益補償也
有所提升。整體配置上，在利率仍具不確定性的環境下，
可偏向中短天期，並依風險承受度與收益需求擇優布局。

## Document 580: 内外経済ウォッチ『日本～春闘は「上げ方」も焦点に～』(2026年12月号)

Source PDF: all report pdf/内外経済ウォッチ『日本～春闘は「上げ方」も焦点に～』(2026年12月号).pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

日本　 
～春闘は「上げ方」も焦点に～
　連合は2027年春闘（春季生活闘争）において、全体の
要求目安を「6％中心・5％以上」とした。5％以上の要求は
これで4年連続だ。労働側の要求と賃上げ実績は、近年概
ねパラレルに動いている。根強い物価上昇と深刻な人手
不足を背景に、来春も高めの賃上げが続く公算が大きく
なっている。
　もっとも、春闘の焦点は「上げ幅」だけにとどまらなく
なっている。連合は2027年、率で示す「上げ幅の指標」に
加え、年齢別の到達賃金で示す「水準の指標」を一段強化
した。初めて2030年までの中期の賃金水準目標を掲げ、
所定内賃金を現行水準から15％程度引き上げ、中位数
で35歳36万円・30歳34万円への到達をめざす。年齢ご
との到達額を示すことで、水準の低い層を引き上げ、格差
を是正しようという発想だ。企業側でもベースアップや初
任給の大幅引き上げ、若年層への重点配分、非正規の時
給改善など、限られた原資を「どの層に厚く配分するか」
が人材戦略に直結する経営課題となっている。
来年の春闘も高い賃上げ率へ
　こうした「上げ方」の違いは、家計の実感にも表れてい
る。勤労者世帯の収入階層別に実質可処分所得の変化を
みると（資料2）、2019年から2025年にかけて、最も低い
層（第1分位、＋3.7％）と最も高い層（第10分位、＋2.5％）
はプラスを確保した一方、第2～第9分位の中間層は総じ
てマイナスとなった。低所得層は最低賃金の大幅な引き
上げ、高所得層は共働き増による配偶者収入の増加など
が効いた。はざまの中間層は、賃上げが物価上昇に追い
つききらず、生活実感が相対的に弱い。連合総研の調査
でも、賃上げが物価高に追いついていないと感じる人の
割合は50代・60代前半で7割弱に達し、20代を大きく上
回る。連合が中期目標で「分厚い中間層の復活」を掲げた
背景もここにある。
　一昔前のような「一律ベア」へ全面的に戻ることは、人
材獲得競争が激しさを増すなかで考えにくい。ただ、メリ
ハリの効いた賃上げは、中間層の底上げという課題と背
中合わせでもある。「上げ幅」に加えて「上げ方」も賃上げ
動向を見るうえで重要になってきている。
「U字型」の実質可処分所得
経済調査部　主席エコノミスト　星野 卓也（ほしの たくや）
資料1 春闘賃上げ率と要求水準（2027は目安）
（出所）連合より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所が作成。

資料2 年収階層別・実質可処分所得の伸び
（出所）総務省より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所が作成。
5 第一ライフ研レポート 2026.12
内外経済ウォッチ

## Document 613: 凱基投顧_Takeaway_2636 台驊控股_賴偉中_20261113

Date: 2026-11-13

### Page 1

十一月 13, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

法說重點摘要

台驊控股 
(2636 TT, NT$71.0, 未評等)

重點摘要 
1. 台驊 3Q26 EPS 3.73 元、YoY+30.32%，成長主要來自海運價格提
升帶動。AI 出口暢旺帶動台灣航空貨運需求強勁成長，不過台驊空
運客戶主要是近程線的供應鏈，正積極佈局長程線。 
2. 進入淡季，美線運價已開始下跌，不過管理層認為運價可維持高檔
震盪，接下來還有中國農曆年前小旺季，預期運價下跌幅度不會超
過 30%。 
3. 2027 年海運影響因素主要為 1.)紅海復航(目前聯盟航商已復航
70%航次，若全數復航將釋出約 5~6%運力)、2.)新船下水量大增
（預計超過 300 萬 TEU），公司預期 2027 年運價不會像 2H26 這
麼好，但對 2027 年海運並不悲觀。 
結論 
2026 年台驊營運成長主要來自海運價格提升，雖 2027 年海運價格展
望不如 2026 年，不過今年航商獲利豐沛，配發之現金股利仍可挹注高
額業外收益。以今日收盤價 71.00 元試算，股價約當 3Q26 每股淨值
77.6 元之 0.91 倍。

公司營運概況 
 台驊國際控股(2636 TT ，T3EX)為國際物流承攬集團，業務涵蓋海
運、空運、中歐鐵路、內貿物流、倉儲與報關；公司以「核心物流
平台、策略性產業投資、資本配置、資金管理」四大價值主軸分別
衡量營運表現。 
 股權架構：直接持股台驊物流(THI，海運/鐵路/內貿物流)、台灣空
運 (TEC ，空運/ 報關/ 陸運) 、 THI Japan 及 Skymaster( 韓國) 各
100%；間接持股包括 PDQ(北美，台驊物流持股 100%)、ATP(新
加坡高科技空運與保稅倉，台灣空運持股 88%)、Shipgo/世購國際
(跨境電商物流)、特易行通運(陸運配送)及樺嘉實業(報關)，後三者
由台灣空運持股 100%。 
 公司強調持有之海運公司營運穩定，今年海運公司獲利很好，明年
可收取現金股利值得期待，且公司的財務狀況和現金流是物流同業
中最好的。 
 空運，各家航空公司都有配合，國籍外籍都有，運價不一定國航比
較高，要看機型為主。今年不是運價的問題，不會壓低空運價格，
重點在優化服務。台驊空運主要客戶是近程線的供應鏈，正積極佈
局長程線的空運。

產業概況 
 近期運價看法，預期運價到年底高檔震盪，接下來還有農曆年前小
旺季，預期運價不會跌超過 30%。 
 2026 年海運業獲利展望，2026 年航運三雄財報獲利會是歷史次
高，僅次於 2022 年。4Q26 獲利應該可以維持 3Q26 水準。 
 2027 海運展望，1H27 影響因素主要是紅海復航、新船下水量大
增，明年不悲觀，但不會像 3Q~4Q26 這麼好，尤其 9-10 月美線
運價幾乎來到疫情期間的價格。預期 2027 年運價美國線>歐洲
線，目前看美國通膨問題影響比較大，觀察點是 2Q27，公司認為
川普對外態度會反轉，包括停止軍援烏克蘭、加快美伊停戰等。 
 紅海復航，聯盟船大概 70%復航，約 30%還在繞行好望角（確保
若紅海封鎖，隨時可繞回好望角），若全數復航，運力可以釋放
5~6%。

 新船下水，預計 2027 全年有 300 萬 TEU 新船下水，船公司目前計

以上老船佔比約 6%，如運價大跌，可能會拆船。 
 運力控制，航商現在很熟悉如何控制運力且現金流也穩健，手段包
括停航、空白航班、跳港、減班等，削價競爭狀況變少，公司認為
很難看到長期低運價。

精密，運力調度沒想像這麼容易。

## Document 636: 南ア中銀はインフレ加速下でも利上げを一時停止、ランド相場の先行きは？ ～追加利上げに含みも、米ドル高や金価格の下落、政治_b51f9d9c

Source PDF: all report pdf/南ア中銀はインフレ加速下でも利上げを一時停止、ランド相場の先行きは？ ～追加利上げに含みも、米ドル高や金価格の下落、政治_b51f9d9c.pdf
Report source: DLRI

Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026年11月 20日

1/3 
南ア中銀はインフレ加速下でも利上げを一時停止、ランド相場の先行きは？ 
～追加利上げに含みも、米ドル高や金価格の下落、政治的不透明感がランド相場の重しに～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ 南アフリカ準備銀行（SARB）は 11月 19日の定例会合で、政策金利を 7.25％に据え置くこと
を決定した。イラン情勢の悪化に伴う原油高を背景に、10 月のインフレ率は前年同月比＋
5.2％、コアインフレ率も同＋4.2％と、ともに目標レンジ（３±１％）を上回っている。 
○ しかし、過去２回の利上げによる金融引き締め効果や景気の先行きを巡る不透明感を踏まえ、
SARBは様子見姿勢に転じた。今回の決定では７人の委員のうち３人が利上げを主張するなど
意見が割れた。ハニャホ総裁は、インフレリスクが上方に傾いているとの認識を示し、必要
に応じて追加利上げを実施するタカ派姿勢を維持した。 
○ 金融市場においては、ＦＲＢの追加利上げ観測による米ドル高や金価格の下落がランド相場
の重しとなっている。SARBの様子見姿勢に加え、統一地方選挙での最大与党ＡＮＣの退潮を
受けた政策運営の不透明感も、先行きのランド相場の足かせとなる可能性がある。

【インフレ率は目標レンジを上回るも、SARBは２会合ぶりの金利据え置きで様子見姿勢に転じる】 
南アフリカ準備銀行（SARB）は、11月 19日に開催した定例の金融政策委員会において、政策金
利を 7.25％に据え置くことを決定した。SARBは、2024年９月にコロナ禍後初の利下げに舵を切り、
その後も一時休止を挟みつつ、断続的に利下げを進めてきた。その背景には、ここ数年の同国の経
済成長率が、2023年（＋0.8％）、2024年（＋0.5％）、2025年（＋1.1％）と３年連続で１％程度に
とどまるなど、他の新興国と比べて見劣りしていることがある。さらに、2024年以降はインフレ率
が鈍化し、物価環境が落ち着いたことも、断続的な利下げを後押しした。しかし、イラン情勢の悪
化に伴う原油などの価格上昇を受けて状況は一変した。 
同国は原油や石油製品の大部分を輸入に依存しており、原油輸入の約４分の１をサウジアラビア
に、石油製品輸入の半分以上をオマーン、ＵＡＥ（アラブ首長国連邦）、バーレーンなどの中東諸国
に依存している。このため、イラン情勢の悪化に伴う原油高は、輸入額の増加による対外収支の悪
化やインフレの加速など、経済のファンダメンタルズ（基礎的条件）の脆弱化につながるとともに、
供給懸念の高まりが幅広い経済活動に悪影響を及ぼすことが懸念された。４月のインフレ率が SARB
の定める目標レンジ（３±１％）を上回ったことから、SARBは５月の定例会合で約３年ぶりの利上

### Page 2

2/3 
げに舵を切った。その後はコアインフレ
率も目標レンジを上回る伸びに加速し

行きを巡る不透明感を理由に金利を据

コアインフレ率ともに一段と加速した

ジを上回る伸びに加速している（図１）。こうした状況にありながら、SARBは今回、２会合ぶりに
金利を据え置くなど、様子見姿勢に転じた。

【委員の間で意見が割れるなか、ハニャホ総裁は追加利上げに含みを持たせる考えを示す】 
会合後に公表された声明文では、今回の決定について「７人の委員のうち４人が据え置きに賛成
し、３人が 25bpの利上げを主張した」と、委員間で意見が割れたことを明らかにした。そのうえ
で、足元の世界経済について「依然として厳しい状況にある」とし、「地政学リスクを背景に原油価
格は高止まりし、食料インフレも加速している」ほか、「主要国の中銀は引き締めバイアスを維持し
ており、さらなる利上げが見込まれる」との見方を示した。また、「世界経済と貿易は底堅く、ＡＩ
（人工知能）ブームの恩恵を受けている国もある」とする一方、「世界的に財政状況がひっ迫してお
り、債務の持続可能性を巡る不透明感が高まるとともに、借入コストに上昇圧力がかかる動きがみ
られる」とした。 
同国経済については「成長見通しはほぼ変わらない」としつつ、「緩やかな回復が進んでいるとみ
られる」とした（図２）。そのうえで、先
行きの経済成長について「足元の１％強
から２％程度まで加速が見込まれる」も
のの、「投資の低迷による下振れリスク
がある」との見方を示した。物価動向に
ついては「エネルギーインフレを主因に
加速している」とし、「2027年初めまで
高止まりが続く」ものの、その後は「エ
ネルギーインフレの影響が一巡する一
方、食料インフレやランド安による輸入
物価の上昇が影響するため、鈍化ペース
は緩やかなものにとどまる」との見方を示した。インフレ見通しについては「様々なリスクがある」

として、「ドル高、地政学リスクによるエネルギー価格への影響、エルニーニョ現象による影響」を

今回の決定については「過去２回の利上げにより金融政策スタンスはやや引き締め的になってい

うえで、「リスクが現実化すれば利上げを躊躇しない」、「インフレ率を３％の目標水準に戻すこと
に引き続き取り組む」と強調し、タカ派姿勢を維持する考えを示した。

【米ドル高や金価格の下落がランド相場の重しとなるなか、政治を巡る不透明感にも注意】 
このところの金融市場では、ＦＲＢ（米連邦準備制度理事会）による追加利上げが意識されるな
か、米ドル高圧力が強まっている。さらに、足元では金の国際価格が調整の動きを強めており、金
価格に連動する傾向があるランド相場
の下落要因となっている（図３）。こうし
たなか、SARBが追加利上げに含みを持た
せつつも、インフレの加速が確認されて
いるにもかかわらず、当面は様子見姿勢
を維持する考えを示したことは、ランド
相場の重しとなる可能性がある。加え
て、同国では今月４日に実施された統一
地方選挙において、最大与党ＡＮＣ（ア
フリカ民族会議）のさらなる退潮が鮮明
となった。今後は、今回躍進したＤＡ（民
主同盟）などとの連立による国民統一政府（ＧＮＵ）の運営に影響が及ぶなど、政策運営を巡る不
透明感が高まる懸念もある。こうした政治的な不透明感も、ランド相場の足かせとなると見込まれ
る。

核心观点 
2026 年 11 月 23 日 
加息预期升温与避险情绪交织， 
全球风险资产延续调整 
 海外方面，美国10月核心零售销售及进口价格超预期，显示需求
仍具韧性、通胀压力持续，美联储会议纪要及官员讲话进一步强
化加息预期，市场定价12月加息预期为93%。AI方面，尽管英伟
达业绩及营收指引表现强劲，Anthropic创纪录IPO，但均未能扭
转科技股抛售趋势，加息预期上升进一步加剧估值压力。地缘方
面，中俄美三国发布和平宣言，释放大国关系趋稳的信号。上周
全球风险偏好显著降温，加息预期升温与避险交易叠加，纳指、
标普500及道指分别下跌约10.0%、8.6%和8.0%，费城半导体指数
重挫17.1%；尽管美联储加息预期强化，但避险需求推动10Y美债
收益率下行约20BP至4.94%，美元指数维持在103.9附近高位，强
美元、流动性压力及需求担忧共同压制金属价格，黄金、铜价周
跌幅均在9%左右，布油回落至92美元。本周关注美国10月PMI、
PCE、三季度GDP修正值。 
 国内方面，10月经济增长动能边际放缓，投资疲弱与内外需分化
进一步凸显。工业增加值同比增长4.8%，较上月回落0.7%，高技
术制造业及出口保持较高景气，继续支撑生产端增长；消费同比
增长0.4%，汽车消费下滑构成主要拖累，服务消费相对稳健。1-
10月固定资产投资降幅扩大至8.1%，房地产、基建及制造业投资
同步走弱，民间投资持续收缩，反映实体经济内生投资动力仍然
不足。上周A股加速调整，沪指跌2.8%至3533点,中证1000跌超
10%、创业板跌超8%，两市日均成交额降至1.42万亿元，超5200
只个股周五收跌，市场风险偏好明显降温。行业方面，银行、食
品饮料等防御板块相对抗跌，传媒、机械设备、通信等前期热门
成长板块大幅回调。调整主要受到海外AI投资回报担忧、美联储
加息预期升温及国内基本面修复偏弱等因素共振影响，叠加成交
缩量和资金撤出，市场负反馈进一步加剧。周末监管层推出降低
融资保证金比例、创业板引入做市商制度及一揽子降费措施，释
放明确的稳市信号，有助于缓解流动性压力、提振市场风险偏好。
叠加前期市场大幅调整，预计本周A股超跌反弹概率提升，但海外
市场压力尚未消除，政策底向市场底的传导仍需观察，重点关注
成交量回升、增量资金入场及反弹持续性。 
 风险因素：全球央行货币政策紧缩超预期；地缘冲突及欧美政治
风险升级；国内经济复苏及政策效果不及预期。

一、海外宏观 
1、10 月美国零售仍具韧劲 
美国零售增速明显放缓，核心消费仍具韧性。10 月美国零售销售环比增长 0.2%，符合
预期，较前值0.8%明显回落；剔除汽车、汽油后的零售销售环比增长0.5%，高于预期的0.3%；
更能反映 GDP 消费支出的控制组环比增长 0.6%，高于预期的 0.4%。整体来看，消费总量有
所降温，但核心消费仍保持韧性，尚未出现明显失速。 
耐用品消费分化加剧，地产链及汽车消费承压。10月机动车及零部件销售环比下降1.6%
（前值-0.2%），家具及家居用品下降 2.3%（前值+3.6%），电子及家电销售持平；建材及园
艺用品环比增长 0.5%（前值-0.8%），有所改善。汽车、家具等利率敏感型消费仍然偏弱，
地产链修复基础尚不稳固。 
非耐用品消费结构分化，线上渠道及个护消费表现较强。非店铺零售（主要为电商）环
比增长 1.2%，医疗及个人护理用品增长 1.5%，杂项零售增长 4.7%；加油站销售增长 1.5%，
但服装、食品杂货等品类有所回落。整体来看，美国消费呈现总量放缓、结构分化特征，名
义零售增长尚有韧性，但剔除通胀后实际销售量有所下降，居民实际购买力仍面临压力。短
期消费韧性对经济形成支撑，但通胀粘性也可能限制美联储降息空间。

二、国内宏观 
1、10 月经济数据延续降温 
10 月社零增速再度回落，居民消费内生动能仍显不足。10 月社会消费品零售总额同比
增长 0.4%，较 9 月回落 0.7 个百分点，季调环比仅增长 0.02%；剔除汽车后社零增长 2.5%，
较前值 3.9%明显放缓。结构上，汽车类零售同比下降 21.4%，降幅继续扩大；家具类下降
2.6%，建筑装潢材料下降 11.2%，地产后周期消费仍然疲弱，家电类增长 2.5%，受以旧换新
政策带动有所改善。服务零售额 1—10 月同比增长 4.9%，明显好于商品零售。整体来看，
消费呈现商品偏弱、服务相对稳健的结构性分化，政策支持尚未有效转化为广泛的消费回暖，
居民内生需求修复仍显不足。

10 月工业生产增速回落，新动能支撑进一步增强，传统行业仍显疲弱。10 月规模以上
工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，较 9 月回落 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.21%，较前值 0.61%明显
放缓；分行业看，制造业增长 5.4%，采矿业和电力热力燃气及水生产供应业分别增长 0.7%
和 4.4%。结构上，高技术制造业同比增长 17.6%，较上月加快 2.3 个百分点，装备制造业增
长 10.6%，其中计算机、通信和电子设备制造业增长 19.4%，科技制造仍是主要支撑；相比
之下，非金属矿物制品、黑色金属及有色金属冶炼分别下降 2.3%、1.7%和 4.4%，传统周期
行业持续承压。整体来看，工业生产延续新动能强、传统产业弱的分化格局，出口及科技制
造支撑生产韧性，但内需不足仍制约传统行业修复，供强需弱的矛盾尚未根本改善。 
图表 6 工业增加值同比增速

图表 7 10 月工业增加值细分行业同比增速 
资料来源：iFinD，铜冠金源期货 
固定资产投资降幅进一步扩大，地产、基建及制造业投资同步走弱。1-10 月固定资产
投资同比下降 8.1%，较 1-9 月的-7.2%降幅扩大 0.9%，10 月单月环比下降 2.93%，投资动能
明显转弱。分领域看，房地产开发投资下降 21.6%，降幅扩大 1.0%；制造业投资下降 2.9%，
降幅扩大 0.8%；基础设施投资下降 5.1%，降幅扩大 0.9%。民间投资下降 11.4%，降幅扩大
1.0%，反映企业投资意愿仍然不足。结构上，高技术产业投资增长 4.0%，其中信息服务业、
航空航天及电子通信设备制造业保持增长，成为少数亮点。整体来看，投资端呈现传统领域
全面承压、新兴产业局部支撑的格局，内生投资需求仍然疲弱，企稳基础尚不牢固。 
房地产市场调整持续深化，销售、投资及资金端压力进一步加大。1-10 月新建商品房
销售面积同比下降 13.7%，销售额下降 14.3%，降幅较 1-9 月分别扩大 0.5 和 0.4%，销售端
持续走弱；房地产开发投资同比下降 21.6%，降幅扩大 1.0%。房屋新开工、施工和竣工面积
分别下降 24.4%、13.0%和 26.6%，其中竣工降幅扩大 1.5%，开发及交付活动仍在收缩。房
企到位资金同比下降 21.9%，其中国内贷款下降 33.4%，定金及预收款下降 15.5%，融资与
销售回款压力持续。目前地产销售疲弱、资金收缩与投资下行仍在相互强化，房地产市场尚
未出现实质性企稳信号。

## Document 667: 宏观周报：经济数据维持供强需弱

Source PDF: all report pdf/宏观周报：经济数据维持供强需弱.pdf
Report source: 华联期货
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 96
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

华联期货宏观周报
经 济 数 据 维 持 供 强 需 弱
20261122
作 者 ： 石 舒 宇
期货从业资格号：F03117664

审核：姜世东，期货从业资格号:F03126164，期货交易咨询从业证书号：Z0020059

### Page 3

周度观点及策略

### Page 4

周度观点
• 工业生产保持较快增长，多数行业实现增长。1—10月份，全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长5.3%，保持较快增长态势。10
月份，全国规模以上工业增加值增长4.8%，剔除季节因素后环比增长0.21%。从三大门类看，采矿业、制造业、电力热力燃
气及水生产和供应业分别增长0.7%、5.4%、4.4%。从行业看，41个工业大类行业中28个行业增加值增长，增长面为68.3%。
• 装备制造业支撑有力，产业结构持续改善。10月份，规模以上装备制造业增加值同比增长10.6%，保持两位数增长；累计增
加值占全部规模以上工业比重为37.9%，同比提高1.8个百分点，产业结构持续改善。装备制造业中的8个行业增加值全部实
现增长，其中，电子、仪器仪表行业增速高位继续加快，增速分别为19.4%、14.2%，较上月分别加快3.0个、0.5个百分点；
专用设备、铁路船舶航空航天、金属制品、通用设备、电气机械行业分别增长11.5%、9.2%、8.6%、8.5%、8.1%，实现较快
增长。从产品看，医疗仪器设备及器械、民用钢质船舶、数控金属切削机床等重点装备产品产量分别增长39.6%、35.6%、

23.8%。
• 高技术制造业创近五年最高增速。10月份，规模以上高技术制造业增加值同比增长17.6%，增速较上月加快2.3个百分点，
为近5年来各月最高增速。从行业看，电子工业专用设备制造、计算机外围设备制造、电子器件制造、光纤光缆及锂离子电
池制造等行业增加值分别增长46.8%、44.7%、34.8%、22.0%。从产品看，“人工智能+”高速发展带动集成电路、电子元件、
光电子器件等产品产量分别增长21.9%、15.2%、15.0%；工业机器人、电子工业专用设备等高端装备产品产量分别增长
26.0%、17.5%。
• 1—10月份，全国固定资产投资（不含农户）364824亿元，同比下降8.1%，民间固定资产投资同比下降11.4%，降幅均有扩
大。工业投资同比下降3.6%。其中，采矿业投资增长2.5%，制造业投资下降2.9%，电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业投
资下降7.7%。从环比看，10月份固定资产投资（不含农户）下降2.93%。

### Page 5

周度观点
• 科技创新和产业创新融合持续深入，新动能加快成长壮大，高技术产业投资力度不断加大。1—10月份，高技术产业投资同
比增长4.0%，拉动全部投资增长0.4个百分点。高技术制造业投资同比增长3.0%。其中，电子电路制造业投资增长59.6%，
增速比1—9月份加快0.9个百分点；锂离子电池制造业投资增长18.0%；集成电路制造业投资增长13.8%；电子专用材料制造
业投资增长8.2%。
• 两重”项目建设扎实推进，“六张网”相关领域投资建设加快规划实施，带动重点领域基础设施投资较快增长。1—10月份，
互联网和相关服务业投资同比增长48.4%，增速比1—9月份加快1.7个百分点；航空运输业投资增长12.5%；水上运输业投资
增长12.5%；电力供应业投资增长9.9%。
• 1—10月份，社会消费品零售总额416464亿元，同比增长1.0%。扣除汽车类商品后的消费品零售额增长2.8%，增速与前三季
度持平。乡村市场增长持续好于城镇，1—10月份，包含镇区和乡村的县乡消费品零售额同比增长1.9%，增速明显快于城区

水平。其中乡村消费品零售额56546亿元，增长2.2%，增速连续15个月快于城镇。
• 1—10月份，商品零售额同比增长0.9%。在限额以上单位中，近六成商品类别零售额实现增长，五类商品零售额增速在5%以
上。基本生活类商品销售增势平稳。1—10月份，限额以上单位粮油食品类和饮料类商品零售额分别增长6.1%、4.9%，今年
以来保持稳定增长；服装鞋帽针纺织品类、日用品类商品零售额分别增长3.8%、3.4%。
• 消费品以旧换新政策持续见效，相关商品零售较快增长。1—10月份，限额以上单位通讯器材类、文化办公用品类零售额在
去年较高基数的基础上同比分别增长16.5%、6.0%。以旧换新补贴叠加“双十一”促销，带动部分商品销售改善。10月份，
限额以上单位家用电器和音像器材类零售额增长2.5%，增速比9月份加快0.6个百分点；体育娱乐用品类零售额增速由降转
增，增长1.0%；以可穿戴智能设备、高能效等级家电为代表的智能、绿色商品零售额持续快速增长，增速均比9月份有所加
快。

### Page 6

周度观点
• 2026年10月份，70个大中城市中，一线城市商品住宅销售价格环比总体上涨，二三线城市环比下降，一二三线城市同比降
幅继续收窄。
• 10月份，一线城市新建商品住宅销售价格环比由上月上涨0.1%转为持平。其中，上海和深圳分别上涨0.2%和0.1%，广州持
平，北京下降0.1%。二线城市新建商品住宅销售价格环比由上月持平转为下降0.1%。三线城市新建商品住宅销售价格环比
下降0.2%，降幅与上月相同。70个大中城市中，新建商品住宅销售价格环比上涨城市有14个，比上月减少3个。
• 10月份，一线城市二手住宅销售价格环比上涨0.2%，涨幅与上月相同。其中，上海、广州和深圳分别上涨0.5%、0.2%和
0.1%，北京持平。二线城市二手住宅销售价格环比下降0.3%，降幅比上月扩大0.1个百分点。三线城市二手住宅销售价格环
比下降0.3%，降幅与上月相同。70个大中城市中，二手住宅销售价格环比上涨城市有7个，比上月减少2个。
• 10月地产开发投资同比降幅达21.6%，住宅投资降幅与整体基本持平，房企开发意愿偏弱。施工面积、新开工面积、竣工面

积全线负增，新开工下滑24.4%、竣工下滑26.6%，竣工端跌幅甚至大于开工，反映两个核心矛盾：一是房企拿地与新增开
工意愿持续低迷，对未来销售预期偏谨慎，主动压缩新增供给；二是保交楼压力仍存，项目交付节奏受阻，存量在建项目
推进乏力。住宅分项跌幅普遍高于整体，说明本轮调整的核心载体仍是住宅，房企优先收缩住宅类资本开支，供给端处于
持续去产能的阶段，短期新房供给增量有限。
• 新房销售面积、销售额双双双位数下滑，住宅销售面积降幅更深，新房市场需求仍偏弱，购房者观望情绪较重。结构上最
大亮点来自二手房：1—10月二手房网签面积同比增长11.4%，二手房交易活跃度显著高于新房，体现市场需求结构切换，
刚需和改善需求更多转向存量房，以价换量、交易流程更灵活的二手房承接了一部分购房需求。库存层面，10月末新房待
售面积小幅同比回落1.7%，尤其是3年以下短期库存下降5.3%，短期去库节奏有所改善，但要注意待售口径不含大量已售未
交付、停工缓建的存量项目，库存指标改善不能等同于市场需求回暖，只是狭义现房库存边际缓解。

规上工业电力生产有所下降。9月份，规上工业发电量8294亿千瓦时，同比下降1.2%；日均发电276.5亿千瓦时。1—9月份，规上工业发电量74916亿千瓦时，同比增长2.2%。
9月份，规上工业火电降幅收窄，水电由增转降，核电、风电、太阳能发电增速加快。其中，规上工业火电同比下降1.4%，降幅比8月份收窄2.9个百分点；规上工业水电下降
18.1%；规上工业核电增长21.5%，增速加快12.1个百分点；规上工业风电增长10.6%，增速加快2.7个百分点；规上工业太阳能发电增长19.5%，增速加快9.2个百分点。

1—9月份，全国规模以上工业企业实现利润总额60337.4亿元，同比增长15.5%（前值15.7%）。采矿业实现利润总额8813.7亿元，同比增长38.2%；制造业实现利润总额
45738.0亿元，增长17.0%；电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业实现利润总额5785.6亿元，下降14.3%。
主要行业利润情况如下：计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业利润同比增长103.8%，有色金属冶炼和压延加工业增长79.7%，煤炭开采和洗选业增长57.0%，化学原料和化学
制品制造业增长49.2%，石油和天然气开采业增长21.3%，纺织业增长7.2%，专用设备制造业下降1.3%，电气机械和器材制造业下降5.9%，农副食品加工业下降17.3%，电力、
热力生产和供应业下降17.9%，汽车制造业下降22.8%，非金属矿物制品业下降46.9%，黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业下降73.3%，石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业由亏转盈。

### Page 25

CPI和核心CPI
图：CPI 图：核心CPI
资料来源：WIND、华联期货研究所 资料来源：WIND、华联期货研究所
2026年10月份，全国居民消费价格同比上涨0.7%。其中，食品价格下降2.9%，非食品价格上涨1.5%；消费品价格上涨0.8%，服务价格上涨0.7%。1—10月平均，全国居民消费
价格比上年同期上涨0.9%。
10月份，食品烟酒及在外餐饮类价格同比下降1.6%，影响CPI（居民消费价格指数）下降约0.48个百分点。食品中，鲜菜价格下降16.9%，影响CPI下降约0.31个百分点；鲜果
价格下降3.8%，影响CPI下降约0.07个百分点；畜肉类价格下降2.7%，影响CPI下降约0.11个百分点，其中猪肉价格下降8.6%；蛋类价格上涨10.1%，影响CPI上涨约0.05个百
分点。

价格下降0.7%，耐用消费品价格上涨1.4%。

进口金额 进口金额同比（右轴）
海关总署披露数据显示，按美元计价，2026年10月我国出口4123.3亿美元，同比增长35.2%，较上月小幅下降1.1个百分点；进口3070.5亿美元，同比增长41.6%，较上月加快

10.6个百分点。
出口结构高度分化，AI硬件板块贡献突出。10月集成电路、自动数据处理设备出口金额同比分别增长137.1%、114.8%，增速环比分别上行1.1、21.8个百分点，两类产品合计
拉动当月出口增速13.6个百分点，贡献近四成出口增量，是本轮出口高增的核心支柱。与之形成对比，汽车出口增速明显收敛，由9月62.0%回落至37.6%，核心原因是前期面
向欧盟的“抢出口”红利逐步消退。市场此前对中欧混动汽车贸易新规存在政策不确定性预期，多家车企选择在规则落地前集中发运、提前报关，透支了阶段性订单，造成
基数扰动，10月回归常态节奏后增速回落。

固定资产投资额
图：三大产业固定资产累计投资额同比 图：建筑安装工程和设备工器具购置累计投资额同比
资料来源：WIND、华联期货研究所 资料来源：WIND、华联期货研究所
2026年1—10月份，全国固定资产投资（不含农户）364824亿元，同比下降8.1%，降幅较上月增加0.9%。1—10月份，民间固定资产投资同比下降11.4%。从环比看，10月份固
定资产投资（不含农户）下降2.93%。分产业看，第一产业投资7018亿元，同比下降6.1%；第二产业投资142333亿元，下降3.6%；第三产业投资215473亿元，下降10.9%。
工业投资同比下降3.6%。其中，采矿业投资增长2.5%，制造业投资下降2.9%，电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业投资下降7.7%。基础设施投资同比下降5.1%。其中，信息
传输业投资增长32.0%，航空运输业投资增长12.5%，水上运输业投资增长12.5%。

### Page 68

货币流动性
从货币供应指标来看，10月末广义货币M2余额同比增长7.1%，增速较9月小幅回落0.1个百分点，较去年同期下行1.1个百分点，整体保持温和扩张态势，边际略有走弱；狭
义货币M1余额同比增长4.4%，环比回升1.2个百分点，但同比仍低于上年同期1.8个百分点。对应M2-M1剪刀差收窄至2.7个百分点，剪刀差收敛，反映出企业活期存款占比
边际改善，资金从定期、储蓄类存款向企业经营活期端转移，企业经营活跃度边际回暖。外部流动性层面形成阶段性支撑：近期我国贸易顺差维持高位、持续走高，叠加
人民币汇率走出升值行情，企业净结汇规模明显扩张，外汇占款渠道带来增量流动性，成为托底M2增速的重要外部因素。

汇率
国家外汇管理局统计数据显示，截至2026年10月末，我国外汇储备规模为34052亿美元，较9月末上升49亿美元，升幅为0.14%。中国10月末黄金储备为7830万盎司，较9月末
的7747万盎司增加83万盎司，为连续第24个月增持黄金。2026年10月，受全球宏观经济数据、主要经济体货币政策及预期等因素影响，美元指数上涨，全球主要金融资产价
格涨跌互现。汇率折算和资产价格变化等因素综合作用，当月外汇储备规模上升。

### Page 85

中国制造业PMI
图：中国制造业PMI热力图
资料来源：WIND、华联期货研究所
2026年10月份，制造业采购经理指数（PMI）为49.3%，比上月下降0.8个百分点，制造业景气水平有所回落，修复动能阶段性走弱。
从分类指数看，生产分项大幅回落至49.8%，采购量、进口分项同步下滑，企业主动下调生产节奏、缩减原料采购与海外中间品进口。10月新订单回落至49.4%，内需转弱；新
出口订单下滑至48.7%，外需边际降温；企业在手订单进一步降至44.1%，创3月以来新低，制造业企业存量订单持续消耗，需求韧性不足。虽然出厂价格、原材料购进价格仍维
持在荣枯线以上，但涨幅较9月明显收窄，上游原料涨价动能放缓，工业品价格的上行边际减弱，企业成本端压力有所缓和。
高技术制造业和装备制造业PMI分别为51.3%和50.6%，继续高于临界点，相关行业企业生产经营保持扩张，新质生产力延续良好发展态势。消费品行业和高耗能行业PMI分别为
48.8%和47.3%，比上月下降1.9个和0.7个百分点，市场活跃度有所减弱。

### Page 87

中国非制造业PMI
图：中国非制造业PMI热力图
资料来源：WIND、华联期货研究所
2026年10月份，非制造业商务活动指数为49.7%，比上月下降0.5个百分点，非制造业景气水平有所下降。
分行业看，建筑业商务活动指数为48.0%，比上月下降2.3个百分点；服务业商务活动指数为50.0%，比上月下降0.2个百分点。从服务业行业看，铁路运输、水上运输、
邮政、互联网软件及信息技术服务等行业商务活动指数均位于55.0%以上较高景气区间；资本市场服务、保险、房地产等行业商务活动指数均低于临界点。
需求端是非制造业PMI回落的核心拖累，内外订单同步走弱。10月新订单回落至44.4%，内需订单明显收缩；新出口订单下滑至46.5%，外需同样偏弱，叠加在手订单
43.8%持续处于低位，存量订单储备不足。销售价格回落至49.5%，重回荣枯线下方，终端提价能力走弱；投入品价格回落至51.5%，虽然仍维持扩张，但上游成本上行
力度有所放缓，成本端压力边际缓和。

服务业商务活动指数位于临界点。服务业商务活动指数为50.0%，比上月下降0.2个百分点，服务业景气度稳中略降。从行业看，主要服务业门类商务活动指数均有回升，
其中与“十一”假期出行消费相关的铁路运输、住宿、文化体育娱乐等行业商务活动指数回升明显，且均位于扩张区间，市场活跃度提升；水上运输、邮政、互联网软件
及信息技术服务等行业商务活动指数均位于55.0%以上较高景气区间，相关企业业务总量增长较快；仅金融行业商务活动指数回落且幅度较大，这也是本月服务业景气度略
有回落的主要原因。建筑业商务活动指数为48.0%，比上月下降2.3个百分点，建筑业施工活动环比有所放缓。

## Document 728: 法人税収の伸びはなぜ名目GDP成長率を上回るのか

Source PDF: all report pdf/法人税収の伸びはなぜ名目GDP成長率を上回るのか.pdf
Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

財政

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9番 1号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和
証券グループ本社を親会社とする大和証券グループの会社です。内容に関する一切の権利は㈱大和総研にあります。無断での複製・転載・転送等はご遠慮ください。

2026年 11月 18日 全6頁

法人税収の伸びはなぜ名目 GDP成長率を上回
るのか 
資産価格の上昇が課税所得を押し上げている可能性

経済調査部 主席研究員 末吉 孝行

[要約] 
◼ 2023～25年度において、国の税収の伸びを名目 GDP成長率で割った税収弾性値は平均
1.6と試算され、政府が後年度影響試算で使用する1.2を上回る。とりわけ法人税の税
収弾性値は平均3.4と高い。 
◼ 法人税の税収弾性値が高いのは、課税所得が名目 GDPを上回って伸びているためであ
る。海外子会社からの配当の増加のほか、資産価格の上昇に伴う株式などの売却益が
課税所得を押し上げている可能性がある。 
◼ 「責任ある積極財政」を掲げる高市早苗政権は、消費税減税や防衛力強化など様々な施
策を実行するにあたり、財源のひとつとして税収の上振れ分を充当するとしている。
ただし、資産価格の上昇が継続するとは限らず、法人税収がこれまでのような高い伸
びを続けるかどうかも不透明である。税収の上振れを財源として活用する際には、そ
の要因が持続するかについて慎重に見極める必要があろう。

### Page 2

1

2 / 6

「責任ある積極財政」のカギを握る税収増 
高市早苗政権は「責任ある積極財政」を掲げ、経済成長によって税率を上げずとも税収を増
加させることで、財政の持続可能性を高めることを目指している1。その際によく言及されるの
が「税収弾性値」だ。一般的には「税収の伸び率÷名目 GDP成長率」と定義され、経済成長と
税収を関連付ける数値として使われることが多い。 
予算編成時に、政府が翌年度の税収見通しを作成する際には詳細な計算が行われるため、必
ずしも税収弾性値が使われるわけではない。一方で、政府は今後数年間の財政状況について
「後年度歳出・歳入への影響試算」で示しており、そこでは税収弾性値を1.2として将来の税収
を比較的簡便に算出している2。 
しかし、実際の税収弾性値は毎年度の振れが大きく、1.2を上回ることも多い。制度変更の
影響を除いて 2023～25年度の税収弾性値を計算すると 0.3～2.6で、平均 1.6であった（国の
一般会計ベース、図表 1左）。 
また、税目によって税収弾性値は異なる。国の税収の大部分を占める所得税、法人税、消費
税3の基幹三税について過去 3年平均を見ると、税収弾性値は所得税 1.6、法人税 3.4、消費税
1.1である。中でも法人税収の伸びが、名目 GDP成長率を大きく上回っている。

図表 1 税収弾性値（国の一般会計、左）、名目GDPと税目別の税収の推移（00年度＝100、右）

（注）各年度の税収（決算ベース）から、予算策定時における税制改正による増減収見込み額を差し引き、そ
れを税制改正がなかった場合の税収額として、各年の税収の伸びを算出することで、税制改正による増減税の
影響を除いている。税収はいずれも国の一般会計ベース。 
（出所）財務省、内閣府統計より大和総研作成

1 例えば、首相官邸「第 221回国会における高市内閣総理大臣施政方針演説」（2026年2月 20日）を参照。 
2 財務省「令和 8年度予算の後年度歳出・歳入への影響試算」（2026年2月） 
3 所得税、法人税、消費税の 3つで国の一般会計の税収の 87％を占める（2025年度決算ベース）。
